# Changelog

Release notes are published on [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases),
which is the canonical source for the complete release history. This file highlights the current
release train and preserves selected historical development notes.

## [v0.134.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.134.0) — 2026-10-30

Deletion and erasure correctness: deleting a user removes the soft-deleted workspaces they owned instead of refusing, erasure audit sweeps touch an address only while the member held it, erasure and user deletion take their row locks in one order, and session rollback no longer writes stale or disallowed values. Version 0.133.0 was not released; its one change shipped in v0.132.0.

### Changed

- **User deletion removes soft-deleted owned workspaces** ([#2406](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2406)): `DELETE /api/v1/admin/users/{user_id}` and `cli.delete_admin` hard-delete the soft-deleted workspaces the user owns, with everything still in them (other members' contexts and memories included), write one `workspace_purged` audit row each with context and memory counts, and list them under `purged_workspaces`. The `409` "Restore and transfer soft-deleted owned workspaces first" is gone, and naming one of those workspaces or its contexts as a transfer target is refused. The API deletes without a preview; both CLI prompts list the workspaces with counts. Account erasure no longer waits for a successor for a soft-deleted workspace. `create_admin` refuses a login ID whose `local:` user id still owns a workspace left by an earlier deletion.
- **Erasure audit sweeps match an address only while it was held** ([#2359](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2359)): the live erasure and `reprocess_erasures` rebuild the periods the member held each email address from the `oauth_user_email_synced` audit rows, so rows written while another account held the same address are left alone, and addresses the member used before an email sync are pseudonymized too. When a period cannot be established the rows stay raw: erasure summaries count them under `postgres.audit_mentions_kept` and `audit_addresses_not_recovered`, and `reprocess_erasures` prints the same counts.
- **One lock order for erasure and user deletion** ([#2418](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2418)): erasure holds the subject's `users` row and takes workspace rows without waiting (short retries up to 0.75 s), so it can no longer deadlock with `DELETE /api/v1/workspaces/{id}`. A busy row reschedules the request with `503` `ERASURE-007` (`details.error_class` `LockNotAvailable` or `DeadlockDetected`) on the same attempt budget as vector-store failures, then `failed` with `409` `RES-002`. Admin user deletion answers a deadlock with the existing `409`; `delete_admin` asks for a re-run. `DELETE /api/v1/workspaces/{id}` locks the affected users right after the workspace row.

### Fixed

- **Session rollback payload sync** ([#2346](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2346)): a group whose re-read fails is rolled back and skipped instead of being patched from the values queued at restore time, and a failed group commit is rolled back so the next group reads normally. The `rollback_session` reply no longer fails after a rolled-back step.
- **`rollback_session` checks the restored type** ([#2432](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/pull/2432)): a restore that would write back a memory type the caller may not write is skipped as `not_permitted`, and the dry run shows no restored fields for it.

### Notes

- No migration, no new environment variables. `AUDIT_HMAC_KEY` must be the key the email-sync audit rows were written with; addresses written under an earlier key stay raw and are counted as not recovered.
- **After deploy:** run `reprocess_erasures --all` (plan), check the "kept" and "not recovered" counts, then `--all --apply --yes` once. A second plan can show a higher not-recovered count, because the first apply pseudonymized the plaintext it recovered.
- **Leftover workspaces:** run the read-only query in `docs/deployment.md` ("Workspaces left by accounts deleted before v0.134.0"). Delete soft-deleted leftovers with the documented block and re-own live ones; until then `create_admin` refuses colliding login IDs.
- **Monitoring:** `erasure_retry_scheduled` with a lock `error_class` means a workspace operation raced an erasure; a request that ends `failed` with `lock_conflict after 4 attempts` deleted nothing in Postgres, but its vector points are already gone and any workspace hand-overs from the workspaces step stay committed; request the erasure again to finish it.

## [v0.132.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.132.0) — 2026-10-29

Authorization and credential correctness: OAuth / MCP tokens move to hashed storage through a staged migration, OAuth forms refuse repeated parameters and dynamic client registration keeps only the redirect URIs that pass validation, context-less views and `supersedes` edges follow the same access and ownership rules as the rest of the API, and capacity-purge notices match the real deletion date.

### Added

- **Hashed OAuth / MCP tokens** ([#2399](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2399)): access and refresh tokens can be stored as SHA-256 digests. The upgrade starts in a `bridge` mode that still writes raw tokens; storage becomes hash-only only after an operator runs the finalizer described under Notes, and a database copy or backup taken before that still holds usable tokens. Token values, lifetimes, rotation and revocation are unchanged for clients. Bearer, refresh, introspection and revocation look a token up by its digest or by its raw value, so unconverted rows keep working, and they refuse a presented digest. OAuth token fragments are removed from REST / MCP logs; API-key prefixes are still logged as before.

### Changed

- **Owner rule for `supersedes` edges** ([#2313](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2313)): MCP `create_edge` / `update_edge` and REST graph creation require both memories to belong to the caller (a linked account counts only in a private context), so a shared-context editor can no longer hide another member's memory from default recall. MCP answers `permission_denied`; REST keeps its endpoint `404` checks and answers `403` `AUTH-101` for the ownership refusal. Retyping an edge away from `supersedes` stays allowed. The accept action in the memory dialog is gated separately in [#2427](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2427).
- **OAuth form handling** ([#2238](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2238)): authorization, token, device, revocation and introspection routes refuse a repeated single-valued parameter, empty duplicates included, and answer a malformed or oversized form with a generic `invalid_request`. Authorization error pages carry the consent page's cache and framing headers. An unknown `token_type_hint` is still ignored, as RFC 7009 §2.2 requires.
- **Dynamic client registration** ([#2238](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2238)): a registration accepts at most 100 redirect URIs, drops the entries that fail pattern or provider validation, and answers `invalid_client_metadata` only when none survive. Manual client editing stays strict. Redirect URIs are logged without userinfo, query, fragment or provider binding segments.

### Fixed

- **Context-less views follow the context access list** ([#2325](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2325)): the memory list, stats and access patterns without a `context_id`, and the workspace stats and timeline, count only contexts the member can open; losing a plan seat removes that reach and restoring it brings it back. A scoped API key's dashboard uses the key's own workspace. Context names, creators and health counts come only from reachable contexts; the rest is one row labelled "Not accessible".
- **Capacity-purge notices** ([#2321](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2321)): when notices start late, the 7 / 3 / 1-day notices follow the actual deletion deadline instead of the lock's age, and a missed or failed final notice postpones the deletion. A premature final record written by an older worker no longer authorizes deletion.

### Notes

- **Migration `f20_2399_oauth_token_storage`** starts token storage in a `bridge` mode that still writes raw values, because the deploy runs Alembic before the old API processes stop. Hash-only storage needs an operator step after every old reader and writer has drained: run `PYTHONPATH=src python -m auth.oauth_token_storage --writers-drained`, then check the counts and the constraint as `docs/ops/oauth-token-hash-rollout.md` describes. Fresh installations need the same finalizer. A downgrade after hashing is refused.
- **Capacity-purge sweep workers**: update every sweep worker before enabling the purge; an old worker still applies the old notice and deletion rules. No feature flag changes.
- No new environment variables.

## [v0.131.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.131.0) — 2026-10-28

Workspace ownership and connector key integrity: deleting a user no longer leaves a workspace, a context or a connector key without a live owner.

### Changed

- **Administrative user deletion requires successors** ([#2372](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2372)): `DELETE /api/v1/admin/users/{user_id}` answers `409` listing the owned workspaces (`workspace_ids`) and created contexts (`context_ids`) that still need a surviving successor; the retry names them in the request body (`workspace_transfers` / `context_transfers`). The transfers, cleanup and audit rows commit together, and other authors' memories and connector write targets survive. A workspace under a running subscription refuses with `BILLING-005` ([#2417](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2417)).
- **`delete_admin` hands the admin's workspaces over** ([#2371](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2371)): a live workspace the admin owns that other users are in goes to `--transfer-to USER` (or a per-workspace `--workspace-transfer WORKSPACE=USER`), the new owner is notified, one only the admin is in is deleted with it; the command runs on one async session, re-checks after the prompt and refuses while a subscription runs. Contexts the admin created pass to their workspace owner; a soft-deleted workspace the admin owns is left as it is, contexts included ([#2417](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2417); follow-up [#2406](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2406)).
- **Private contexts stay personal** ([#2417](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2417)): a private context nobody else wrote in is deleted with the user instead of being handed to a successor; one a linked account wrote in passes to that account ([#1784](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1784)); one holding another account's memories, and a connector's write target, take a successor like a shared context.

### Fixed

- **Key rotation during a deletion** ([#2370](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2370)): deletion and member removal lock the departing user before re-owning connector keys, and rotation locks the candidate owners before the key rows, so a rotation can no longer mint a replacement into the re-own / delete gap.
- **Lock order of admin deletion and the sole-owner cascade** ([#2347](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2347)): identity lock, then contexts, then memories in id order, with the subject's edited-only revisions in the first union.
- **Admin users screen** ([#2417](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2417)): the delete toast says why a delete was refused.
- **Storage quota after a deletion** ([#2417](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2417)): the quota release skips the workspaces deleted in the same cascade, so a hard-deleted workspace no longer gets a negative Redis counter.

### Notes

- No migration, no new environment variables. An admin user delete through the API now takes the request body described in `docs/deployment.md`; the admin users screen deletes only users who own nothing until the successor picker lands ([#2420](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2420)).

## [v0.130.1](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.130.1) — 2026-10-28

Patch release: Meta's Muse can register as an MCP client through dynamic client registration.

### Fixed

- **Meta Muse DCR callback** ([#2410](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2410)): `POST /api/v1/oauth/register` accepts Muse's single callback `https://agent.meta.ai/api/hatch/oauth/callback`, compared whole and as written; `muse.ai`, other `meta.ai` hosts and scheme, case, port, path, query and fragment variants stay refused. The registration rejection message names the callback, and the API reference lists it.

### Notes

- No migration, no new environment variables, no operator action. Muse registers from Meta's servers under the existing 5/min/IP registration limit; an observed egress range can be exempted with `OAUTH_DCR_RATE_LIMIT_EXEMPT_CIDRS`.

## [v0.130.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.130.0) — 2026-10-27

Account erasure now keeps surviving memories outside search until an administrator explicitly restores their scrubbed index, while preserving resource indexes another live, non-suppressed memory still uses. Vector and attachment cleanup retain retry work across failures. API key regeneration rotates the key selected in the web UI.

### Fixed

- **Erasure logs omit raw account identifiers** ([#2375](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2375)): vector deletion logs record a subject digest and exception class instead of the raw subject or SDK exception text.
- **Regenerate the selected API key** ([#2379](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2379)): the credentials UI sends the selected key ID. Rotation preserves its agent binding, default context and exact expiration, leaves other keys alone, and serializes competing rotations. Connector keys still rotate through their connector. Calls without `key_id` retain the legacy newest-active-key selection.
- **Erasure scrubs audit metadata and legacy actor fallbacks** ([#2348](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2348)): exact subject matches in six known top-level metadata fields, and legacy raw-sub / `<sub>@api` actor values, are pseudonymized without rewriting unrelated fields or another live account's values. `reprocess_erasures` repairs historical rows, including legacy JSON that cannot be cast to JSONB.
- **Stale erasure runs cannot delete a re-registered account** ([#2362](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2362)): the executor rechecks the original account row and the request's committed Postgres marker before destructive work. A replaced run preserves the completed run's counts and does not repeat subject-wide cleanup against a new account.
- **Attachment deletion survives workspace cascades and storage outages** ([#2395](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2395)): deleted file registry rows enqueue object cleanup in their database transaction. A 15-minute sweep retries storage deletion, respects outstanding upload URLs and preserves objects any other registry row still references. Workspace deletion releases attachment quota; historical unregistered objects have a separate counts-first inventory command.
- **Bridge availability during deployment** ([#2408](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2408)): API blue/green deployments and rollbacks no longer restart the co-resident bridge worker by default, avoiding the restart-induced interruption to its public endpoint, including Slack event webhooks.

### Changed

- **Erased-author indexing requires an explicit restore** ([#2391](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2391)): a durable suppression marker excludes erased-author survivors from ordinary retries, model changes, chunk repair and Sleep. Captured UUID scopes make post-commit vector cleanup retryable, including after the request finishes; the hourly sweep also rechecks completed scopes for late writes. Shared resource points remain available while another live, non-suppressed row uses them. An administrator can restore scrubbed search data with a scoped `include_erased=true` retry.
- **Erasure-aware background writes and dashboard counts** ([#2397](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2397)): background indexing checks the current author and suppression state so stale work cannot restore an erased author's points. The dashboard reports intentionally unindexed erased memories separately from failed or stalled indexing, using the existing context visibility rules. Those memories alone do not raise the indexing backlog warning.

### Migration

Two additive migrations are required, in order:
`f18_2395_file_cleanup` → `f19_2391_embedding_erasure`
(after `f17_2298_audit_logs_user_id_idx`). Run `alembic upgrade head` using the normal deployment procedure. f18 adds the file cleanup outbox, registry index and triggers. f19 adds durable embedding suppression and vector cleanup scopes, and backfills the previous exact erased-author marker in batches.

Adds the optional deployment-host environment variable `BRIDGE_RESTART_ON_SWITCH` (default: `0`). Operators using an older bridge worker that reads the active-color marker only at startup must set `BRIDGE_RESTART_ON_SWITCH=1` in the deployment script's shell environment for both deployments and rollbacks. Setting it only in the API's environment file does not configure the script. Workers that follow active-color changes require no configuration change.

During the rollout, pause account erasures and the repair CLIs. Update and drain the old API, scheduler, embedding, Sleep and legacy attachment-migration processes before running repairs; older writers do not enforce the new suppression and cleanup policy. Use the API's existing database, storage and vector configuration, and keep the existing audit pseudonym salt.

From `backend/`, review each read-only plan before applying:

1. Repair historical request and audit metadata first:
   `python -m src.cli.reprocess_erasures --all`
   then `python -m src.cli.reprocess_erasures --all --apply --yes`.

2. Repair historical erased-author survivors and vector points:
   `python -m src.cli.cleanup_erased_vectors`
   then `python -m src.cli.cleanup_erased_vectors --apply --yes`.
   Ambiguous author cohorts remain unchanged and are reported as counts. Explicitly restored memories are not suppressed again. Vector-store failures leave durable jobs for the hourly sweep.

3. For attachment objects orphaned before f18:
   `python -m src.cli.sweep_orphan_objects`
   then `python -m src.cli.sweep_orphan_objects --apply --yes`.
   First drain old legacy attachment writers and wait out any previously issued, longer-lived presigned PUT URLs. The command considers only recognized application keys with no registry owner and an age of at least two days, or the configured PUT lifetime plus one hour, whichever is longer. Apply rechecks ownership and age. Versioned buckets need a separate lifecycle policy for noncurrent versions.

Ordinary `POST /api/v1/admin/embedding/retry-failed` now skips erased-author survivors. To intentionally restore their scrubbed search index, specify a context or workspace filter and `include_erased=true`; for example:
`POST /api/v1/admin/embedding/retry-failed?context_id=<uuid>&include_erased=true`.
A context embedding-model change no longer restores those memories.

### Notes

For a code rollback, keep the additive schema changes in place. A schema downgrade removes durable state: f18 drops the storage cleanup queue and triggers; f19 drops all vector cleanup scopes and the suppression/restoration columns.

If a schema downgrade is unavoidable, pause erasure, indexing and repair workflows first. Preserve every storage cleanup queue row, every vector cleanup scope (pending and completed), and each memory UUID's `embedding_suppressed_at` and `embedding_erasure_restored_at` values. Restore that state before resuming those workflows. Re-applying f19 does not reconstruct it: the backfill recognizes only the previous exact erased-author marker. Completed scopes are needed to detect late writes, and explicit restoration markers prevent historical repair from suppressing an intentional restore.

Older code does not enforce the new suppression policy; keep erasure, indexing and repair workflows paused while it runs.

## [v0.129.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.129.0) — 2026-10-27

Account erasure works per `details` key: the keys an erased member introduced go back, also where a later member's edit carried them, and only those keys leave the revision log. A failed erasure puts the member's embeddings back in the queue. A completed one keeps the sweep from recreating its points. A connector's write key now survives member removal and the admin user delete as well. Migrations wait at most 5 s for a table lock, and `deploy.sh` retries a lock timeout. `?add_tools=` widens the default tool list, and `create_edge` joins the core profile (22 tools). No migration; one operator plan run of `reprocess_erasures --all` follows the deploy.

### Added
- **`?add_tools=` on the MCP URL** ([#2354](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2354)): adds comma-separated tool names to the profile the URL selects (`core` when none is given), e.g. `?add_tools=list_edges` or `?profile=core&add_tools=list_edges,merge_contexts`. Names are read like `?tools=`: trimmed, case-sensitive, de-duplicated, at most 100. Unknown names are ignored and logged, and a value that matches no tool is not an error. `?tools=` still wins and is never widened: `add_tools` next to it is ignored and logged as `mcp_tool_profile_add_tools_ignored`. `profile=full` already lists everything. `describe_tools` treats added tools as listed and names `?add_tools=` in its URL hints, as do the hidden-tool hints and skills. `GET /api/v1/memory/guardrails/digest?target=instructions` takes an optional `add_tools` so its preview matches such a URL. The profile stays a view: `tools/call` never reads it.
- **`create_edge` in the core profile** ([#2353](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2353)): a default-URL client can link two memories as `supersedes` or `related_to` without a `describe_tools` round trip; the default list has 22 tools. `update_edge`, `delete_edge` and `list_edges` stay out. The core list keeps its size budget: the edge-type list and existing-pair rules moved to the new guide topics `create_edge.types` / `create_edge.existing`, and the nested Returns detail of `reference`, `remember` and `bootstrap` moved to their guide topics.
- **`connector_id` on API key responses** ([#2299](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2299)): `GET /api/v1/config/api-keys` and the member credentials list and mint responses carry `connector_id` (null for an ordinary key). The web UI uses it, not the `connector:` name, to tell connector keys apart. A renamed connector key still offers no default-context control, and its Regenerate action is hidden (it is rotated from its connector).
- **Migration lock timeout** ([#2300](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2300)): every migration transaction starts with `SET LOCAL lock_timeout`. An `ALTER` that waits behind a long transaction then fails with SQLSTATE `55P03` instead of queueing every later query on the table. The new environment variable `KAGURA_MIGRATION_LOCK_TIMEOUT` sets the cap: default `5s`, a PostgreSQL duration (a bare number is milliseconds), `0` for no cap. An invalid value stops `alembic` before any schema change. A revision's own `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` applies only until that revision ends. Statements inside an autocommit block (`CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY`) are not capped. `deploy.sh` re-runs a migration that failed on a lock timeout after 15 s and then 45 s, three attempts in all, logging each retry (`MIGRATION_RETRY_DELAYS`, default `"15 45"`). Any other failure aborts at once, as before. `docs/deployment.md` (*Migration lock timeout*) shows how to find the blocking session.

### Fixed
- **Erasure reverts carried-forward `details` keys** ([#2275](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2275)): on a memory another member authored, the erasure replays the memory's revision log per top-level `details` key.
  - A key whose live value the erased member introduced goes back to its value from before their write, or is removed when it had none. This now also covers a key a later member's whole-dict edit carried unchanged.
  - A key a later member rewrote stays.
  - **Deletions stand:** a key the erased member removed stays removed. Before, the whole-dict revert put it back when their edit was the newest.
  - In the revision log only the member's keys leave each prior. The survivors' keys stay, so a rebuilt version (`reference(version=N)`, `list_revisions`) shows them instead of marking `details` unavailable. The removed keys are recorded as SHA-256 digests in the prior's new `erased_details_keys` marker (never the names), so two erasures give the same result in either order. Version rebuilds and session rollback ignore the marker.
  - The erasure's own revision row now holds the overwritten value without the member's keys, plus `erased_details_keys` and `restored_from`. It also maps each restored key (by digest) to the version it went back to (`details_restored_from`, #2393), so a later erasure that replays it credits each key correctly; the row is written even when the restored value equals the live one.
  - Memories the member authored still lose `details` whole.
  - New counts in `deleted_data_summary.postgres`: `details_keys_reverted` (keys put back or removed) and `details_keys_ambiguous`. The second counts live keys left in place because an erasure before v0.129.0 stripped the member's state whole, so their value cannot be told from a later member's own; they are never reverted on a guess. `revision_details_stripped` now counts rows stripped per key.
  - `reprocess_erasures` applies the same rules to past erasures. It prints `details keys ambiguous  <request id>  N key(s) kept (stripped history, see runbook)`, which is not a change: applying it writes nothing.
  - A survivor's pending session rollback over a memory the erasure reverted is skipped `modified_since`, because the erasure's version is a later write.
- **Embeddings re-queued after an erasure fails before its Postgres commit** ([#2358](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2358)): when the vector delete succeeded and the request then failed before the Postgres commit (`workspace_transfer_required`, an error inside the Postgres step), the account stays but its points may be gone. The same applies when a retried request fails for another reason (a billing contract that started while it waited, a configuration error at the vector step). In both cases the member's live embedded memories go back to `pending`, as after a cancelled or exhausted retry (#2304), once the `failed` row is written. `retry.attempts` is 0 when the vector step never failed. A failure after the Postgres commit re-queues nothing. An admin force-erase runs the re-queue before it answers 409.
- **Erased member's queued memories are not re-embedded** ([#2363](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2363)): the erasure's Postgres step takes all the member's surviving memories out of the embedding queue, including ones already embedded (#2393).
  - They end `failed` with `embedding_error` starting `erased_author:` and the retry budget spent, so the embedding sweep no longer recreates their points under the pseudonym. The count is `deleted_data_summary.postgres.memories_embedding_dequeued`.
  - An embedding run in flight that finds its claim lost and the row's author changed (or the row gone) deletes the point it wrote (`embedding_points_removed_after_lost_claim`, or `embedding_points_removal_failed` if that delete fails). The re-queue of a finished newer run now checks the author in the same UPDATE, so a stale run cannot put an erased row back in the queue (#2393).
  - Right after the commit the member's points are deleted once more, which removes a point written between the vector delete and the row locks. This is best-effort: the counts are in `qdrant_after_commit`, and a failure is recorded as `qdrant_after_commit_failed`, logged as `erasure_late_points_delete_failed`, and the request still completes. The sweep that closes an erasure interrupted after its Postgres commit repeats this delete (#2363 follow-up).
  - `embedding_unsearchable_backlog` counts these rows as `erased`, and they alone do not raise it.
  - Only `POST /api/v1/admin/embedding/retry-failed` or an embedding-model switch of their context brings them back, embedded under the pseudonym with no `details`. A context merge, an admin context restore and a later erasure's `details` restore keep them `failed` (#2393).
- **Erasure order and stale embedding runs** ([#2393](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2393)): found in the v0.129.0 release review. Two erasures over the same memory now give the same `details` in either order, also when a key was deleted and re-added with an earlier value, or when the restored value equals the live one. A stale embedding run that writes after an erasure removes its point and cannot re-queue the erased row.
- **Connector keys kept on member removal and the admin user delete** ([#2352](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2352)): removing a member, `DELETE /api/v1/admin/users/{id}` and `cli.delete_admin` hand the user's live connector write keys to the workspace's owner, as account erasure has done since #2305, instead of deleting them; the worker's stored key keeps verifying. One shared helper (`services/connector_key_ownership.py`) does it with id-ordered locks. Member removal touches only the workspace being left. Keys in workspaces the user owns are still deleted with those workspaces. The admin delete response adds `"reowned": {"connector_api_keys": n}`, and its `deleted.api_keys` no longer includes the re-owned keys.
- **One `resource_id` format on REST and in the web UI** ([#2284](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2284)): `PUT /contexts/{id}` and the context settings form accept hyphens (`^[a-z0-9_-]+$`, at most 255 characters), as the MCP tools and connector provisioning already did. A trailing newline is refused everywhere. Re-sending a context's current slug is a no-op. The duplicate messages of `setup_resource` and the REST update say the slug is unique on the server (it may belong to another workspace's context).
- **Member credentials error text** ([#2202](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2202)): the web UI's member-credentials calls show the API's `message` (then a plain `detail`), falling back to `<action>: HTTP <status>`, instead of the bare status text.

### Changed
- **Store-is-the-authority rule at session start and in `recall`** ([#2341](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2341)): with a usable cache, the plugin's SessionStart hook prints `Kagura Memory: The store is the authority: …` as its first line, ahead of the guardrail line and the memory index. The `recall` description carries the same rule: a stored value outranks general knowledge, and when rows disagree the later `observed_at` (else `created_at`) wins.
- **Plan-tier overrides applied on the first `get_settings()`** ([#2276](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2276)): importing `config.plan_tiers` no longer builds the settings. `PLAN_*` overrides are applied when the settings are first built and cached only once they succeed. An invalid `PLAN_<KEY>_FEATURES` still refuses to boot, now from `get_settings()`. The one-shot CLIs no longer need a pre-import guard.
- **Logging, Qdrant client and test isolation** ([#2241](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2241)): `api.main` configures logging only if nothing in the process did; uvicorn workers are unchanged. The async Qdrant client is bound to its event loop like the Redis client and is closed at shutdown (bounded at 5 s, `qdrant_close_failed` on error). `UserRepository.list()` orders by id, so its skip/limit pages are stable.
- **Shared migration helpers and lint** ([#2240](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2240)): `db/migration_helpers.py` provides `validate_constraint` (VALIDATE in a transaction of its own under the migration lock timeout), `session_lock_timeout` and `index_is_invalid`. AST checks require new revisions to use them and keep every `CONCURRENTLY` statement inside an autocommit block. A design note records that `memory_revisions` retention may prune only whole logs per memory.
- **Frontend lint and coverage in CI** ([#2202](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2202)): `npm run lint` runs ESLint (`eslint-config-next`), and the frontend CI job runs it. Errors fail the job, and the warning count is capped so new warnings fail too. `npm run test:cov` works (`@vitest/coverage-v8`).

### Notes
- No migration (`alembic heads` is still `f17_2298_audit_logs_user_id_idx`). One new environment variable: `KAGURA_MIGRATION_LOCK_TIMEOUT` (default `5s`). `deploy.sh` reads `MIGRATION_RETRY_DELAYS` (default `"15 45"`).
- Operator action (details in `docs/ops/erasure-runbook.md` §2.4 and `docs/deployment.md`, *Erasures run before v0.124.0*): after the deploy, with the vector store reachable, run `python -m src.cli.reprocess_erasures --all` once, without `--apply`, and read the `details keys ambiguous` lines and the `would change` total. Ambiguous keys are never changed by `--apply`. A non-zero count is an input to a follow-up decision. Apply the other planned changes only once that has been decided.
- Behaviour changes to know about:
  - An erasure leaves keys the member deleted deleted and reverts carried keys. Rebuilt versions show the surviving keys (#2275).
  - An erased member's queued memories stay `failed` (`erased_author:`) until an admin retry-failed (#2363).
  - A failed erasure costs one embedding call per re-queued memory (#2358).
  - Member removal and the admin user delete move connector keys to the owner, and the admin delete's `deleted.api_keys` count excludes them (#2352).
  - A migration that waits more than 5 s for a lock now fails and must be re-run; `deploy.sh` retries it, other runners must (#2300).
  - `?add_tools=` and `create_edge` change what the default `tools/list` can show (#2353, #2354).
- **Deploy pause (#2393):** while old and new API, scheduler and CLI processes run side by side, pause erasures (including admin force-erase) and `reprocess_erasures`; resume once the old processes have drained.
- Rolling back to v0.128.0 needs no schema downgrade, and the new summary keys and response fields are additive. But once an erasure has written a revision row with `details_restored_from`, do not run an older erasure executor (v0.128.0 or a v0.129.0 pre-release build) against the database: it ignores the per-key origins and can restore keys in the wrong order. If you must roll back, keep erasures and `reprocess_erasures` paused until v0.129.0 is back.
  - The dequeued `erased_author:` rows stay `failed`, and v0.128.0 counts them under `other` in `embedding_unsearchable_backlog`.

## [v0.128.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.128.0) — 2026-10-26

Account erasure finishes what it started: the sweep patches the vector payloads of a run interrupted after its Postgres commit and never runs an old request against an account registered again under the same identity; other actors' audit rows that name the erased member are pseudonymized with their own; a cancelled or exhausted vector-store retry puts the member's embeddings back in the queue; a connector's write key survives the erasure of the admin who provisioned it. `reprocess_erasures` applies the new rules to past erasures and repairs the last request rows that still stored an account id. Migration **f17** adds one index built `CONCURRENTLY`; one operator run of `reprocess_erasures --all` follows the deploy.

### Fixed
- **Erasure sweep follow-ups** ([#2302](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2302)): right after the hourly sweep closes a run interrupted after its Postgres commit (`after_postgres_commit: interrupted: sweep`), it patches the vector payloads of the memories that run's restore put back (found from the restore's own `memory_revisions` rows, bounded by the run's `memory_scrub_at`) and records the counts in `deleted_data_summary.postgres.details_payload_*`; a patch that fails re-queues the memory for embedding. The sweep decides "this run erased its subject" from the `postgres` summary, not from the user lookup: a member who signed up again with the same identity before the sweep reached the row is never run against — the row is closed the same way, the new account's rows and Redis state are kept, and the closed row no longer blocks the new account's own request (`erasure_sweep_subject_reregistered`). The never-ran close is guarded in SQL against a late Postgres commit, and the pre-commit failure handlers also require `status = in_progress`, so a run that outlived the stale cutoff can no longer overwrite the completion of the run that replaced it. `reprocess_erasures` repairs note rows written before v0.125.0 (`complete` + `user_row_already_absent` with the raw account id): the derived pseudonym, cleared request metadata, `initiated_by` replaced only when it was the member's own id, `"pseudonymized": true`; it re-drives the payload patch once for every `after_postgres_commit:` request whose summary has no `details_payload_synced` (the sweep's own patch interrupted, a row closed before v0.128.0, or a run that failed inside its payload step) and records the counts, so a second run changes nothing. New log events: `erasure_sweep_payloads_resynced`, `erasure_sweep_payload_resync_failed`, `erasure_sweep_subject_reregistered`, `erasure_finalize_request_missing`.
- **Erasure pseudonymizes the member in every audit row** ([#2298](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2298)): rows other actors wrote that name the subject by an exact value — `resource = 'user:'||<email>` or `'user:'||<sub>` (an admin acting on the member) and `user_email = <email>` (the member acting as an admin on someone else) — get the same salted pseudonyms as the member's own rows, under one id-ordered lock over both sets inside the erasure's Postgres transaction; nothing else on those rows changes, and no substring of another actor's text is touched. The count is `deleted_data_summary.postgres.audit_mentions_pseudonymized`. `reprocess_erasures` applies the rewrite to past erasures, recovering each address through the requests' email hash and leaving an address a live user holds again (`audit mentions <id> rows kept: the email is in use again`, repeated while it is in use). Migration **f17** indexes `audit_logs.user_id`.
- **Erasure audit rows carry no request metadata of the member** ([#2345](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2345)): the member's own `audit_logs` rows lose `ip_address` and `user_agent` when they are pseudonymized, the `account_erasure` row is written without them (an admin-initiated erasure included), and its `user_metadata.initiated_by` follows the request row's rule — the pseudonym only when it is the member's own id, an admin's id kept. `reprocess_erasures` clears both columns on the rows that carry a past erasure's pseudonym and rewrites `initiated_by` on its `account_erasure` row (`cleared audit request metadata <id> N row(s)`).
- **Embeddings are re-queued after a cancelled or exhausted erasure retry** ([#2304](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2304)): when a request that already spent at least one vector-store attempt is cancelled by the member, or fails after its fourth attempt, the service puts every live memory the member authored that is embedded or being embedded back to `pending` (resource-indexer rows included; a stale `processing` row with no retry budget left is excluded), in id-ordered batches of 500 that wait at most 5 s for a row lock, and records `deleted_data_summary.retry.embeddings_requeued` / `requeued_at`. A re-queue that fails never changes the cancel's answer or the failure's own error; it is logged as `erasure_embeddings_requeue_failed` for the runbook's manual statement. Cost: one embedding call per re-queued memory, worked off by the embedding sweep at its usual rate, older rows first.
- **Connector keys survive the erasure of the admin who provisioned them** ([#2305](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2305)): the member's live connector write keys (`connector_id` set, not revoked) are handed to their workspace's owner inside the erasure's Postgres transaction instead of being deleted with the member's other keys — hash, prefix, name, connector, workspace and expiry unchanged, so the worker's stored key keeps verifying and the next rotation mints under the owner (`deleted_data_summary.postgres.connector_api_keys_reowned`; a sole-owned workspace still goes with its keys). `POST /api/v1/workspace-connectors/{id}/rotate-kmc-key` now also recovers a connector whose key row is gone (an erasure before this release): when no active key is linked to or named after the connector it mints one under the workspace owner, and a key whose owner is no longer a member is replaced by one owned by the workspace owner; the heal waits at most 5 s for its lock and answers **503 `CONNECTOR-003`** otherwise. The owner's credentials list shows a re-owned `connector:<id>` key.
- **Erasure cancel answers and failure-handler run guard** ([#2306](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2306)): a cancel that loses its compare-and-set re-reads the row as the caller's and answers by what it is now — 200 with the cancelled request when a concurrent cancel of the same request won (a double-click), 409 while it runs, 404 when it ended, is gone or is no longer the caller's. The three failure handlers that run before the Postgres commit (billing contract, workspace transfer, generic) write only this run's row (`started_at` equal to its claim) and log `erasure_failure_handler_stale_run` otherwise, so a run that outlived the one-hour stale cutoff cannot mark the newer run's row.
- **Id-ordered locks for the remaining erasure sweeps** ([#2278](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2278)): the erasure locks every context it writes — the ones the member created and the ones they deleted — in one id-ordered phase before any memory, and the private-context hand-over deletes the member's memories in handed-over contexts through one id-ordered statement; contexts before memories, the order edits, session rollback and context restore already take.

### Changed
- **One post-commit payload step** ([#2283](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2283)): session rollback and the erasure's details restore patch vector payloads through one module (`services/payload_sync.py`): groups of 50 rows read `FOR SHARE` in id order, one transaction per group, with the caller's failure policy — log and go on for session rollback, re-queue the memory for embedding for the erasure. A rollback that fails inside the erasure's policy is logged, never raised. No change to any response or summary key.
- **One helper for erasure request pseudonymization** ([#2288](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2288)): the request row's pseudonymized values (`user_id`, `initiated_by` when it is the member's own id, cleared `ip_address` / `user_agent`) come from one function on every path that writes them, and every pseudonym of a member is derived by one function with the deployment's salt. No change to stored values.

### Notes
- Migration **f17** (`f17_2298_audit_logs_user_id_idx`) adds the btree index `ix_audit_logs_user_id` on `audit_logs (user_id)`, built `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS` in an autocommit block: writes to `audit_logs` continue during the build. It can be run again after an interruption (an invalid leftover index is dropped first), does not run offline (`alembic upgrade --sql`), and needs a direct database connection (not a transaction-pooling proxy). The index is invisible to v0.127.0 code, so a blue-green deploy is safe. No new environment variables.
- Operator action, in this order (details in `docs/ops/erasure-runbook.md` §2.5 / §5.4 and `docs/deployment.md`, *Erasures run before v0.124.0*):
  1. Deploy as usual; f17 runs from the new image before traffic moves.
  2. After the deploy, with the vector store reachable, run `python -m src.cli.reprocess_erasures --all` inside the API container (from `backend/`) to plan, then `--all --apply --yes`, once. The plan scans `audit_logs` twice to recover addresses; run it off-peak. Expect, once: `would pseudonymize note row <id>` for every note row closed before v0.125.0, `would resync payloads <id> N memory(ies)` (N often 0) for every request marked `after_postgres_commit:` that has no `details_payload_synced` yet — each of these counts as a change and gets an `account_erasure_reprocess` audit row — `would pseudonymize audit mentions …` and `would clear audit request metadata …` where past erasures left them. `rows kept` lines repeat on every run while the account id or address is in use again. A second `--all` run prints `would change 0`.
- Behaviour changes to know about: a self-service cancel that loses to a concurrent cancel of the same request answers 200 instead of 409 (#2306); after a cancelled or exhausted vector-store retry the member's memories are re-embedded automatically, which costs one embedding call per memory and delays other members' new embeddings while the queue drains (#2304); the hourly sweep makes vector-store calls when it closes an interrupted run (#2302); a re-owned connector key appears in the workspace owner's credentials list and `rotate-kmc-key` can mint a key for a connector whose key row is gone, answering `CONNECTOR-003` under lock contention (#2305); an admin's audit row about an erased member now reads `user:<64 hex>` in `resource`, and an erased admin's actions on other members show the pseudonym in `user_email` (#2298).
- Rolling back to v0.127.0 needs no downgrade: the index is harmless to the old code, every new summary key is additive, and repaired note rows have the shape the old code already handles. Re-run `reprocess_erasures --all` after rolling forward again.

## [v0.127.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.127.0) — 2026-10-26

Agents find and use what is stored. A memory can carry the date its fact held (`observed_at`), with range filters, ordering and a selector header that follow it. A small context shows its whole summary index at session start. The recall and remember skills skip `list_contexts()` on a default context, store facts as stated and read counts and dates from the whole set. Migration **f16** rewrites `memories` and needs a maintenance window.

### Added
- **`observed_at` event time** ([#2330](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2330)): `details.observed_at` holds the date (`2024-03-07`) or UTC datetime (`2024-03-07T10:00:00Z`) a fact held. Accepted input is extended ISO 8601 only: `YYYY-MM-DD`, or `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS` with optional fractional seconds and an optional `Z` / `±HH:MM` offset (a naive datetime is read as UTC, an aware one converted, fractions dropped); basic forms such as `20240307` are refused. `remember`, `remember_batch` items, `update_memory`, REST `POST /remember` and `PATCH /memory/{id}` also take a top-level `observed_at`, stored into `details`; an `observed_at`-only update is a details edit with a new version, and a `details.observed_at` that disagrees with `observed_at` is `validation_error` (422) on every path. `recall` and `list` filter with `observed_after` (inclusive) / `observed_before` (exclusive; a date bound is the start of that UTC day) on Qdrant, LanceDB and SQL alike; `list(order_by="observed_at")` sorts with `NULLS LAST`. Recall rows, `list` items, bootstrap recall rows and `reference` carry `observed_at` when set. The recall LLM selector's row header shows `observed YYYY-MM-DD` when set, else the written date, and prefers the newer of those (prompt `selector-v4`). The web memory detail shows it (ja 観測日時) without shifting a date-only value across time zones.
- **Session index at SessionStart** ([#2334](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2334)): `load_guardrails` gains an `index` lane. When a context holds at most `SESSION_INDEX_MAX_MEMORIES` (default 60, `0` off) live trusted-tier memories, it lists each as `{memory_id, summary (80-char head), summary_cut?, type, authored_by_caller, updated_at}`, newest first; connector-ingested and superseded memories are left out and the binding filter applies. Above the cap `index` and `index_total_available` are `null` and no count runs. The guardrail lanes keep budget priority. The plugin hook (Claude Code and Codex) prints a "Memory index" block after the guardrail line within its render budget, labels rows another member wrote, and ends with "N more — recall to search" when cut. The cache shape is additive, with no format bump.

### Changed
- **Recall and remember skills use the default context** ([#2332](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2332)): when the server instructions name a default context (`?context=<uuid>`), `recall.md`, `remember.md` and the Codex skill skip `list_contexts()` and leave out `context_id`, as `session-start.md` already did.
- **Remember skill: facts as stated, supersedes on update, `observed_at`** ([#2331](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2331)): the skill stores what the material states, without fact-check notes or added dates. It searches before writing an updated fact and passes `supersedes=`, and stamps a dated material's own date in `details.observed_at`.
- **Recall skill: when, how many, which is current** ([#2333](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2333)): counts come from `list(…, include_details=true)` and its `total` (following `next_cursor`), not from the top five hits. Time questions use `observed_after` / `observed_before` and `order_by="observed_at"`. The later `observed_at`, else the later `created_at`, is the current value. The store is the authority.

### Notes
- Migration **f16** (`f16_2330_observed_at`) adds `memories.observed_at` as a STORED generated column from `details` plus the partial index `idx_memories_observed_at`. It **rewrites the whole `memories` table under ACCESS EXCLUSIVE**: run the deploy in a maintenance window after checking the table size (`docs/deployment.md`). The migration waits at most 5 s for the lock and can be re-run after a timeout. It first rewrites parseable legacy `details.observed_at` values to the stored form and logs how many it could not parse.
- After the deploy, run `python -m src.cli.observed_at_backfill` once (without flags it prints the plan; `--apply --yes` writes, `--yes` skips the prompt for a non-interactive shell) so vector points written before f16 carry the `observed_at` payload. Until then range filters on recall miss those points; points with no `observed_at` need nothing.
- New environment variable `SESSION_INDEX_MAX_MEMORIES` (default 60).
- Rolling back to v0.126.0 needs no downgrade: the old code never names `memories.observed_at`, and the generated column fills itself on insert. Leave f16 in place (a downgrade would mean a second full-table rewrite on roll-forward). After rolling forward again, re-run `observed_at_backfill`: points written or edited by v0.126.0 lack or carry an old payload, and the backfill rewrites it from the column for every memory that has one. It never removes a payload, so an `observed_at` removed under v0.126.0 keeps its old value in the vector store until the memory is edited again.

## [v0.126.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.126.0) — 2026-10-25

Authorization is decided against the target: memory, graph-edge and Sleep-report operations check the context they act on rather than the MCP session's workspace, workspace-scoped API keys and member context access lists hold on every path, and the capacity lock is checked only after authorization. Two migrations tie every memory to its context's workspace (f14) and store SQL `NULL` for nullable JSON columns (f15); `update_memory` can accept a `supersede_candidate`; and the capacity-over purge date names the run that purges.

### Added
- **Accept a `supersede_candidate` from `update_memory`** ([#2308](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2308)): `update_memory(memory_id=…, accept_supersede_candidate=true)` writes the `supersedes` edge from the memory to its current suggestion through the same path as `create_edge(edge_type="supersedes")`, clears the suggestion and names the accepted memory as `superseded_memory_id`. It is a graph action only: combined with `dismiss_supersede_candidate`, with field edits or with the `external_id` upsert it is `validation_error`; so is a memory with no live suggestion, a suggestion whose memory was written after the one carrying it, or a pair that is not both the caller's own (the `remember(supersedes=)` rule of #1803; in a private context a linked account's memory counts). A different declared edge already linking the pair is `edge_exists`. `version` and `updated_at` are unchanged. `recall` / `reference` descriptions, `guide`, `docs/mcp-tools.md` and the plugin skills name this as the accept path for core-profile clients; `create_edge` remains the way to link any other pair.

### Changed
- **Memories tied to their context's workspace** ([#1963](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1963)): migration **f14** adds `fk_memories_workspace_context` (`memories (workspace_id, context_id)` → `contexts (workspace_id, id)`, `ON DELETE CASCADE`) on a new unique index `ux_contexts_workspace_id_id`, built `CONCURRENTLY`. Rows whose workspace disagreed with their context's are moved to the context's workspace in batches of 500 (live rows in live contexts re-queued for embedding with a fresh retry budget; `updated_at` unchanged), their graph edges with them, and the constraint is then validated. A memory without a context is unaffected; a context with memories can no longer change workspace. `POST /api/v1/admin/contexts/recover` recovers into the existing context's workspace and answers **400 `REQ-103`** when the request names another one (dry run included); a point whose payload names another workspace is re-queued so its payload is rewritten.
- **SQL `NULL` for nullable JSON columns** ([#2026](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2026)): the 23 nullable JSON / JSONB columns store a Python `None` as SQL `NULL` instead of the JSON value `null` (shared `NullableJSON` / `NullableJSONB` types; `resource_events.event_metadata` and `indexer_state.metrics` keep their `'{}'` default and the plain type). Migration **f15** rewrites the stored JSON nulls of the small tables (`sleep_reports`, `sleep_actions`, `measurements`, `memory_analyses`, `bm25_idf_drift_log`, `erasure_requests`, `workspace_connectors`) in lock-light batches of 1000; `memories` and the large or append-only logs are not rewritten and keep both forms, which every reader treats the same. The erasure summary merge wraps the column in `NULLIF(…, 'null'::jsonb)`, so a summary stored as JSON `null` merges as an object.
- **Capacity lock checked after authorization on MCP** ([#1954](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1954)): the context resolvers apply the lock after membership, privacy, key scope and agent-binding checks, with the policy taken from the dispatcher's classification table. A context the caller cannot read answers `context_not_found`; a caller outside the workspace naming one of its public contexts gets the same, instead of a redacted `capacity_locked` refusal. Authorized callers get the unchanged `capacity_locked` envelope. Tools whose arguments name no context, and the analysis and resource / connector tools, are checked on the session workspace as before; `get_agent_bootstrap` applies the lock in its service.
- **Purge date is the run that purges** ([#1971](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1971)): `capacity_lock.purge_at` on `GET /api/v1/workspace/usage/current`, the web banner and the countdown mails name the latest of day 30 after the lock, a week after the first countdown mail and the day after the final one, never before the next daily run (today's run counts as the next one for an hour past its start). The date is `null` while the purge cannot run (purge flag, countdown-mail flag or a delivering mail provider off). A workspace already counting down may see a later date than before, never an earlier or a past one. Context and backfill file purges share one `purge_live_context_files`.
- **Plan seats split from plan suspension** ([#1956](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1956)): the seat and writable-slot checks live in `services/plan_seats.py` (one `EXISTS` read decides a seat), the paused-refusal builders in `config.plan_tiers`, and read / write access is one `Literal` type. No change to any response.
- **DB-backed tests run in CI** ([#2297](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2297)): the `backend-integration` job runs the whole backend suite against Postgres before the integration suite, and `make test-integration` mirrors both runs.

### Fixed
- **Sleep report tools authorize against the report's context**: `get_sleep_report`, `get_sleep_history` and `rollback_sleep_run` check the context the report belongs to — read access for the reads, the editor or owner role on that context (and not a workspace viewer) for a rollback — with the same agent-binding and workspace-scoped-key checks as the other context tools; a report whose context the caller cannot reach answers `report_not_found`, like a missing one. The capacity lock is checked on the report's workspace after authorization.
- **Member role is enforced on writes against the context's workspace**: memory, edge, agent-state and measurement writes require the member role on the workspace of the context they target, checked in the memory service for REST and MCP alike and in every MCP write tool before the capacity lock. A workspace viewer is read-only, including in a private context it created (it can no longer delete or merge that context, manage its members or create share keys). Declared edges are stored in the context's workspace, so a member whose session points elsewhere creates them. `update_context`, `delete_context`, `update_search_config` and the REST search-config, feedback, memory-stats and duplicates routes honour a workspace-scoped API key.
- **Workspace-scoped API keys are honoured on memory-id, file and tag paths**: `reference`, `list_revisions`, `explore`, `update_memory` / `PATCH /memory/{id}`, `forget` by id and `forget-bulk` judge the key on the memory's own workspace before any membership lookup (another workspace's memory answers not-found); the file tools' `workspace_id` no longer overrides the key's workspace (another workspace answers like a non-member's request, MCP `permission_denied`, REST 403); `list_tags` and `GET /contexts/{id}/tags` resolve the context like the other context reads (key scope, agent binding, member access list); `list_my_bindings` / `describe_binding` list only the key's workspace.
- **A member's context access list applies on every context path**: reads, writes, memory ids, context settings, files, listings and dashboards follow `allowed_context_ids` with one rule — owner and admin reach every context, a member or viewer reaches exactly what its list names, and with no list a viewer reaches every shared context and a member none. A context outside the list answers not-found (REST 404, MCP `context_not_found`); 403 / `permission_denied` is answered only for a reachable context with too low a role. A role change keeps the list; the members page labels an unset list by role. REST `remember` accepts the context id as a string, as `recall` does.
- **`POST /api/v1/memory/forget` by `query`**: with `query` and no `memory_id` the route answers **422 `VAL-001`** (forget by query needs a context; use `POST /api/v1/memory/forget-bulk`) instead of a server error. Nothing was ever deleted by that form.

### Notes
- API surface docs describe the current Sleep report, file tool and `purge_at` behaviour ([#2328](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/pull/2328)).
- Migrations **f14** (`fk_memories_workspace_context`, `ux_contexts_workspace_id_id`, repair of cross-rooted memories and edges) and **f15** (JSON `null` → SQL `NULL` in seven small tables). No new environment variables.
- Operator action, in this order (details in `docs/deployment.md`, sections *Migration f14* and *Migration f15*):
  1. Before the deploy, run the f14 sizing queries: rows that will move, and the receiving workspaces against their plan's memory limit — a move into a Free workspace whose entitlement comes from the billing service can lock it at once (`CAPACITY-001`) and start its purge clock. Confirm `statement_timeout` is not forced on the migration role's session and that no idle-in-transaction session will hold the `CONCURRENTLY` index build; the migration connects directly (a transaction-pooling proxy breaks its session `SET`s and temp tables).
  2. Deploy as usual; f14 then f15 run from the new image before traffic moves. f14 builds one index `CONCURRENTLY`, adds the constraint `NOT VALID`, repairs in batches of 500 and validates; writers on `memories` can stall up to about 10 s at the `ALTER`. A lock timeout, deadlock or interrupted index build fails the migration; run the deploy again and it resumes. Check the f14 log line (`moved`, `re-queued`, `edges`, or "no cross-rooted memories") and the f15 per-table lines.
  3. After `deploy.sh` has stopped the old color, run the seven idempotent f15 rerun statements (one per table, `SET lock_timeout = '5s'`, autocommit) so rows the old color wrote during the window are rewritten too. Re-queued f14 rows drain through the embedding sweep; run `POST /api/v1/admin/embedding/retry-failed` for any that end `failed`.
  4. Optional, separately scheduled: the `memories` normalization procedure (batched `DO` block + `VACUUM (ANALYZE)`), only if exact `IS NULL` counts on `memories.details` / `context` are wanted; it rewrites close to the whole table.
- Behaviour changes to know about: a caller who cannot read a context of a capacity-locked workspace now gets `context_not_found` on MCP instead of a capacity refusal (#1954); `capacity_lock.purge_at` can move later or become `null` for workspaces already counting down (#1971); forget by `query` on REST is 422 instead of a server error; a workspace viewer can no longer write anywhere, including a private context it created; a workspace-scoped key reaches only its own workspace on every path.
- After the deploy a `member` whose `allowed_context_ids` is `NULL` reaches no context on any path, writes included (before only reads refused); a `viewer` with `NULL` keeps every shared context. Set a list with `PUT /api/v1/workspaces/{id}/members/{user_id}/context-access` or on the members page. Context settings, files and memory ids of a context outside a member's list answer not-found (404 / `context_not_found`) where some answered 403 before.
- Rolling back to v0.125.0 needs no downgrade: the f14 constraint is compatible with the old code (only an admin recovery naming a mismatched workspace fails with a server error), and f15's downgrade is a no-op — the old code writes JSON `null` again, which both versions read; re-run step 3 after rolling forward.

## [v0.125.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.125.0) — 2026-10-24

Erasure crash safety and consistency: the audit-log step joins the erasure's Postgres transaction, a run interrupted after its commit is closed and pseudonymized by the sweep, a vector-store outage at the first step reschedules the request instead of failing it, connector keys are linked to their connector by foreign key, and revision timestamps come from one clock.

### Fixed
- **Audit rows inside the erasure transaction** ([#2287](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2287)): the subject's `audit_logs` rows are pseudonymized as the last statement before the user delete, so a failure rolls the whole Postgres step back (request `failed`, retryable) instead of leaving the account id and email on them. The count moved to `deleted_data_summary.postgres.audit_logs_pseudonymized`; `resource` is cut to the column length. `reprocess_erasures` pseudonymizes audit rows an earlier erasure left behind: it finds them by pseudonym, recovers the email through the requests' email hash for every request of the account, never matches a re-registered account, and lists the rows it kept.
- **Erasures interrupted after the Postgres commit** ([#2286](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2286)): the Postgres step records its `postgres` summary on the request row in the same transaction (merged into the existing summary). The hourly sweep, finding a stale `in_progress` request whose user row is gone, marks a run that committed `failed` with `after_postgres_commit: interrupted: sweep` (pseudonymized, counts kept, Redis cleared best-effort) and a run that never started `complete` with the `user_row_already_absent` note, now pseudonymized too. `erased_pseudonyms` includes those rows, `_finalize` clears a stale `failure_reason`, and the re-run scrubs audit residue of pseudonymized note rows. New log events `erasure_sweep_user_row_absent`, `erasure_sweep_redis_cleanup_failed`, `erasure_sweep_request_moved`, `erasure_sweep_close_failed`.
- **Vector-store outage at the first erasure step** ([#2282](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2282)): a `QdrantError` from the vector delete no longer fails the request. It returns to `cooling_off` with `scheduled_for` 15 minutes later and `deleted_data_summary.retry` (attempts, step, error class, time; no ids, no error text), and the hourly sweep runs it again; after 4 attempts it ends `failed` with `vector_store_unavailable after 4 attempts: …`. Admin force-erase answers **503 `ERASURE-007`** with `request_id`, `attempt`, `max_attempts`, `scheduled_for` and `error_class`. The subject cannot cancel an admin request, and the account page shows one waiting for its retry as not cancellable. Points of collections deleted before the failing one stay deleted until re-embedded; the runbook shows the manual re-queue.
- **Cancel vs. the sweep** ([#2282](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2282)): cancelling a self-service erasure request is a compare-and-set on its status. If the sweep claimed the request first, the cancel answers **409 `ERASURE-006`** and the erasure runs.

### Changed
- **Connector keys linked by foreign key** ([#1952](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1952)): migration **f13** adds `api_keys.connector_id` (`ON DELETE CASCADE`, three CHECKs, partial index) and backfills existing `connector:{id}` keys of the same workspace. Key verification, the seat pause, `is_connector_key` and connector deletion follow the link instead of the name. Deleting a connector removes its key row, so the key disappears from the key list instead of showing as revoked. A connector key can no longer be regenerated from the API-keys page or member credentials (**422**; rotate it through the connector), and rotation links a key row left unlinked.
- **One clock for revision timestamps** ([#2135](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2135)): `memory_revisions.at` and `memories.created_at` come from the application clock on every path (`remember`, `remember_batch`, rollback restore), so `rollback_session` windows, `changes_since` and `list_revisions` no longer mix the database and application clocks. Items of one `remember_batch` call no longer share a single `at`.
- **Self-hosted LLM reachability probe** ([#2064](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2064)): the self-hosted LLM provider uses the process-wide cached probe of the embedding path (keyed by base URL and key, failures never cached), so a provider built per request no longer calls `GET /v1/models` every time.

### Notes
- Migration **f13** (`api_keys.connector_id`). No new environment variables.
- Operator action, in this order (details in `docs/deployment.md` and `docs/ops/erasure-runbook.md`):
  1. Deploy as usual; f13 runs from the new image before traffic moves.
  2. After the traffic switch, re-run the idempotent f13 backfill `UPDATE` once, so keys the old color minted during the window are linked, then run the orphan query from the same section.
  3. Before the next account erasure, run `python -m src.cli.reprocess_erasures --all` inside the API container to plan, then `--all --apply --yes`, once.
- Rolling back to v0.124.1 needs no downgrade (the column stays and old code links keys by name); re-run step 2 after rolling forward again. Replicas must share NTP time, as they already must for `updated_at`.

## [v0.124.1](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.124.1) — 2026-10-23

MCP tool annotations: the tools that change who outside the workspace can reach a context now report `openWorldHint: true`.

### Fixed
- **Open-world hint for publishing tools** ([#2290](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2290)): `update_context` (`is_public` publishes a context through the public API) and `setup_resource` (creates a public context and a token for an outside system to write with) now report `openWorldHint: true`. Directory scanners flagged `update_context` as closed-world after the #2279 rewording. The rule is stated once in `docs/mcp-tools.md`: a tool is open-world when it connects to an outside system or when the call itself changes a context's reach. Writes into an already-public context, merges and download links stay closed-world. `readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint` and `idempotentHint` are unchanged.

### Notes
- No migration, no new environment variables. After deploy, rescan the server in the directory portals so they pick up the new annotations.

## [v0.124.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.124.0) — 2026-10-23

Erasure completeness and sweep robustness: erasing a member who authored a time memory no longer fails, the erasure patches vector payloads after its commit and never fails a request on them, one failed request no longer stops the sweep, a request that fails after the Postgres step stays pseudonymized and re-processable, double-erasure chains restore the original author's value, and every erasure write locks its rows in id order.

### Fixed
- **Time memories through erasure** ([#2266](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2266)): erasing a member who authored a `time` memory failed on the `valid_trigger_window_format` check after their vectors were deleted. Such a memory now keeps only its trigger window (`year`/`month`/`day`/`hour`/`minute`/`from`/`until`); the trigger's `text`, the location and every other key are removed. On a memory another member authored, a restored value's trigger is normalized, or the live window is merged in without its `text`.
- **Payloads after the erasure commit** ([#2266](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2266)): restored memories' vector payloads are patched after the Postgres commit, in groups of 50 under share locks, instead of inside the locked transaction. A patch that fails re-queues the memory for embedding and never fails the request. New summary keys `details_payload_synced`, `details_payload_skipped_{tombstone,shared,moved,requeued}`, `details_payload_requeued_after_error`, `details_payload_requeue_failed`.
- **Sweep after a failed request** ([#2266](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2266)): one failed erasure request no longer stops the sweep for the requests after it.
- **Rollback blocks** ([#2266](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2266)): a session-rollback block ends at the first row outside it, whatever field that row changed, and rollback rows record `restored_from`.
- **Failures after the Postgres step** ([#2268](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2268)): a failure in the audit-log, Redis, audit or finalize step ends the request `failed` with `failure_reason = after_postgres_commit: <step>: <ExceptionClass>` (no exception text) and the row pseudonymized like a completed one. The audit-log pseudonymization commits on its own, and logs after the commit carry the request id, step and exception class only.
- **Double-erasure chains** ([#2268](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2268)): when two erased members edited the same memory, the restore walks past every erased member's edits to the original author's value, whichever erasure is processed first.
- **Erasure lock order** ([#2263](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2263)): every multi-row `memories` / `contexts` write of the erasure locks its rows in id order, including the rows the member deleted, so two erasures, or an erasure and a context restore, cannot deadlock. The member's own tombstones are deleted first; new summary key `memory_tombstones_deleted`, and `revision_details_stripped` / `memory_sessions_scrubbed` no longer count those tombstones.
- **`update_context` `resource_id` description** ([#2279](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2279)): the description and the conflict message now say the value names this server's own inbound ingestion endpoint, unique on the server, and that setting it makes no outbound call.

### Changed
- **`reprocess_erasures`** ([#2268](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2268)): the command also covers `failed` requests whose Postgres step committed. A request that failed after that step on an earlier version, and still stores the raw account id, is repaired (pseudonym derived with the deployment's salt, `failure_reason` replaced by the marker) and re-processed. The strip on the member's authored memories is bounded at the new `memory_scrub_at` summary time, else the completion time, else `started_at` + 1 hour. The audit row gains `request_repaired`.
- **One-shot commands** ([#2264](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2264), [#2268](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2268)): every one-shot command runs the production secret guard before opening a database session and names the missing variables instead of printing a traceback. `--yes` without `--apply` now exits 2.
- **Admin CLIs on the host** ([#2264](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2264)): `create_admin` and `reset_password` read `API_KEY_SECRET` and the provider keys with `docker exec` on the pinned container names (`kagura-api`, `kagura-api-blue`, `kagura-api-green`) and refuse when blue and green disagree. The OAuth E2E override guard reads `ENVIRONMENT` through the settings, so the env file counts.
- **CI** ([#2272](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2272)): Playwright 1.64.1; the Playwright jobs run on `ubuntu-latest` again.

### Notes
- No migration, no new environment variables.
- Operator action: after upgrading, run `python -m src.cli.reprocess_erasures --all` once inside the API container to plan, then `--all --apply --yes` off-peak. Before `--apply`, read the `failure_reason` of every `would repair` request in the database; the repair replaces it. See `docs/deployment.md`.
- An erasure request that failed on v0.123.x with `valid_trigger_window_format` in `failure_reason` needs a new erasure request for that account; it completes on v0.124.0.
- Rollback to v0.123.0 needs no data change. While on v0.123.0, erasures of time-memory authors fail again, and requests v0.124.0 marked `after_postgres_commit` are invisible to v0.123.0's re-process command; re-run `reprocess_erasures --all --apply --yes` after upgrading again.

## [v0.123.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.123.0) — 2026-10-21

Erasure completeness and production guards: erasing an account now also puts back the `details` an erased member last wrote on memories other members authored, a one-off command applies today's erasure rules to erasures that ran on earlier versions, the erasure's revision-log updates lock their rows in a fixed order, and the production secret guard now also covers database migrations and the admin CLIs.

### Added
- **`reprocess_erasures` command** ([#2243](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2243)): `python -m src.cli.reprocess_erasures --all` applies today's erasure rules to every completed erasure, using the pseudonym each one stores. It plans by default (`--dry-run`) and writes only with `--apply --yes`; each changed request gets an `account_erasure_reprocess` audit row, and a second run changes nothing. Requests that still store a raw account id are skipped and listed, never used.

### Changed
- **The erasure's revision-log updates are scoped and lock-ordered** ([#2242](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2242)): the details strip only reads revision rows of memories the erased member authored or edited, and the erasure locks those memories, then their revision rows, in id order before it updates them, so it no longer deadlocks against a session rollback or a second erasure.
- **The production secret guard covers migrations and the admin CLIs** ([#2239](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2239)): with `ENVIRONMENT=production`, `alembic upgrade` and `create_admin`, `reset_password`, `delete_admin` and `seed_e2e_admin` refuse development-default or short secrets before touching the database, as the API does. `setup_env` also generates `AUDIT_HMAC_KEY` and `AUDIT_PSEUDO_SALT`; session and device cookies read `ENVIRONMENT` from the settings, so a non-canonical spelling such as `Production` gets `Secure` too.

### Fixed
- **An erased editor's `details` on another member's memory** ([#2244](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2244)): when the erased member made the newest `details` edit of a memory someone else authored, the erasure now puts back the last value another member wrote (walking past the erased member's own consecutive edits; session rollbacks and earlier erasures do not count as anyone's writing), or empties `details` when that value was itself removed by an earlier erasure. It writes a new version with one revision row (`writer: erasure`) and updates the memory's `location` in the vector store; a memory whose embedding had not finished is re-queued.
- **A session rollback no longer exposes another author's value to the strip** ([#2243](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2243)): the details strip judges who wrote each value the same way as the restore, so a rollback the erased member requested no longer causes the value it put back to be stripped.
- **A failed erasure request is marked `failed`** ([#2244](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2244)): it previously stayed in its earlier state after an error.

### Notes
- **Migration** `f12_2244_rev_writer_erasure` widens the `memory_revisions` writer check to allow `erasure` (added `NOT VALID`, validated outside the `ALTER`'s transaction, no table rewrite).
