Downgrading below f12 is refused while any `erasure` row exists. Rolling the API image back to v0.122.0 with the database at f12 is safe until the first erasure on v0.123.0 (or the command's first `--apply`) writes an `erasure` row; after that, v0.122.0's `list_revisions` (REST and MCP) fails with a validation error on every memory carrying one.
- **Operators:** run `python -m src.cli.reprocess_erasures --all` once after upgrading if any account was erased on v0.113.0 through v0.122.x, then `--all --apply --yes`, off-peak (each request holds the erased member's memories locked while it patches the vector store one memory at a time). The vector store must be reachable: a failure rolls that request back and exits 1, and a re-run continues. Until it has run, a revision prior may still hold a value an erased member wrote. The dry run previews each request on its own, so where two erased members edited the same memory the apply can change more than the plan showed; the apply report and audit rows list it. See "Erasures run before v0.123.0" in `docs/deployment.md`.
- **Erasure now rewrites other members' live content** as described under Fixed. A vector-store failure during the restore fails the erasure; a self-service request then needs a new request. A `details` value written before v0.113.0 or by a connector re-index is not in the revision log and is not seen by the restore.
- **Environment:** `JWT_SECRET`, `JWT_ALGORITHM` and `JWT_EXPIRE_MINUTES` are no longer read (nothing has used them since #993); leftover lines are ignored and can be deleted. No new variables. `setup_env` on an existing `.env.local` now generates `AUDIT_HMAC_KEY` / `AUDIT_PSEUDO_SALT` when they are missing or placeholders — on an install that already holds data that is a rotation, with the effects described under "Production secrets" in `docs/deployment.md`. Outside production, an unset `API_KEY_SECRET` now falls back to the public development default with one WARNING, where it previously failed at the first encryption. Operators who run `alembic` or the admin CLIs from a host shell with `ENVIRONMENT=production` need the private secrets in that environment; `deploy.sh` stops at its migration step, before traffic moves.
- **API / MCP:** `list_revisions` (REST `MemoryRevisionItem` and the MCP tool) gains the `writer` value `erasure`; the tool count is unchanged. `deleted_data_summary.postgres` gains `details_restored`, `details_restored_unavailable`, `details_payload_skipped`, `details_embedding_requeued` and `revision_details_stripped`. New audit action `account_erasure_reprocess`; new log events `erasure_details_restored` (INFO), `erasure_revisions_scrubbed` (DEBUG), `erasure_reprocessed` (INFO), and the once-per-process WARNING that `API_KEY_SECRET` is a public development default.

## [v0.122.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.122.0) — 2026-10-21

Recall and supersede correctness: a supersede suggestion on a recalled row stays within the recall's row filters, judge-on recalls resolve suggestions once, a re-embed of unchanged text no longer re-runs supersede detection or the judge, a session's first read past a cut recall result counts as a use of the memory, and the resource indexer isolates a failed event, keeps a user's edits on re-index and writes the same metadata to the point on every path. Also: Gemini is listed as a URL-only MCP client, and Pillow and certifi are bumped for open advisories.

### Changed
- **Supersede suggestions follow recall's row filters** ([#2027](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2027)): on `recall` (MCP and REST), a `supersede_candidate` whose target memory a `type`, `scope`, `source_type`, `source_uri_prefix`, `tags` or `importance` filter keeps out is no longer shown; the row carrying it still is. Time-window filters do not apply to the target, since it is the older fact by definition. The filter runs inside the existing target query, so a recall costs no extra query.
- **A re-embed of unchanged text skips supersede detection** ([#2092](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2092)): a content-chunk backfill, an embedding-model switch or a repair re-queue whose summary and content are unchanged since the row's last successful embed no longer runs the supersede detection or the judge (`SUPERSEDE_VERIFY_MODE=llm`), so a bulk re-queue no longer spends the daily judge cap. An edited memory, a first embed and a retried failed first embed still detect. A stored candidate is left as it is on such a re-embed: an embedding-model switch no longer clears or re-checks it.
- **A session's first continuation read counts as a use** ([#2139](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2139)): `reference(content_offset=…)` (and the other `*_offset` continuations) bumps `reference_count` the first time a session reads that memory, so a memory an agent reads on from a cut `recall` result is counted as adopted. Later pages, past-the-end offsets, historical versions and calls without a session id do not count, and a recall row that shipped content is still not counted. The first-read mark is a Redis key with a 24-hour TTL; a Redis error leaves the read uncounted.
- **Judge-on recalls resolve suggestions once** ([#2131](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2131)): the supersede fold moved to `services/supersede_fold.py`, and the candidates the fold resolves are reused by the page, which no longer queries them a second time. No change in results.

### Fixed
- **Resource indexer follow-ups** ([#2218](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2218)):
  - Each incremental event runs in its own savepoint. A database error in one event no longer rolls back the batch and leaves the resource replaying it; the failed event is skipped and logged, like a Qdrant or embedding failure already was.
  - A re-index of the same document version keeps a user-edited `context_summary` (an edited row, or a row written by `remember`) and `details.location`, in Postgres and in the point payload.
  - A newly created resource row writes `type`, `tags` and `context_summary` to its point, as rebuild and re-index already did, so a `type="resource_data"` filter matches every resource point.
  - The location backfill writes to a resource row's own point, and the boot-time location reconcile no longer deletes `location` from resource points it did not recognise.
- **Pillow 13.0.0 and certifi 2026.10.19** ([#2258](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2258)): transitive dependencies bumped in `backend/uv.lock` for open security advisories (decoder memory-safety issues in Pillow; root CAs with compromised intermediates in certifi).

### Notes
- **Migration `f11_2092_embedded_text_digest`** adds the nullable column `memories.embedded_text_digest` and, in the same transaction, fills it for every row whose embedding succeeded (`md5(summary || chr(31) || content)`). The UPDATE rewrites those rows and holds their row locks until it commits, so on a large store run `alembic upgrade head` off-peak and `VACUUM (ANALYZE) memories` afterwards. Downgrade drops the column. Rows embedded before supersede detection existed, or while the judge was off or capped, are not re-checked by a later backfill; a catch-up pass is tracked in [#2256](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2256).
- **Logs:** new `supersede_detect_skipped_mechanical_reembed` (DEBUG); `embedding_completed` gains `mechanical_reembed`; `knn_seed_skip_no_consumer` with `reason=mechanical_reembed` (DEBUG); `reference_adoption_mark_failed` / `reference_adoption_unmark_failed` (WARNING, Redis); `geo_payload_backfill_shared_point_skipped` (INFO). `supersede_fold_applied` is now emitted by the `services.supersede_fold` logger.
- **Docs:** Gemini's custom MCP connector is listed as a URL-only (DCR) client in the README, `docs/mcp-clients.md` and the web UI ([#2246](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2246)).
- No new environment variables. No API or MCP schema change; MCP tool count unchanged (72).

## [v0.121.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.121.0) — 2026-10-21

Secrets and OAuth hardening, plus test stability: a production deployment refuses to start on the shipped placeholder secrets, Gemini's dynamic client registration succeeds with the redirect URIs that qualify, the revocation and introspection endpoints read a bounded body, a repeated authorization parameter is refused instead of silently resolved, account erasure strips the subject's `details` from the revision log of memories they only edited, older migrations validate their constraints without holding the write lock, erasure lookups on `memory_revisions` get an index, and the Redis client is closed when a worker stops.

### Changed
- **Production refuses development-default secrets** ([#2225](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2225)): with `ENVIRONMENT=production`, the API does not start while `API_KEY_SECRET`, `JWT_SECRET`, `AUDIT_HMAC_KEY` or `AUDIT_PSEUDO_SALT` is still a built-in default or a `change-me-…` / `kagura-erasure-v1` placeholder from `.env.example` or `terraform/single-server/.env.prod.example`, or while one of the first three is shorter than 32 characters. The error in the API log names the variables, never their values. Development, test and staging are not checked. See **Notes** before upgrading.
- **DCR keeps the redirect URIs that qualify** ([#2232](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2232)): `POST /api/v1/oauth/register` now registers the entries of `redirect_uris` that pass the provider rules and leaves the rest out (RFC 7591 §3.2.1), instead of refusing the whole request when one entry fails; the response's `redirect_uris` lists what was registered, and only those are accepted at `/authorize` and `/token`. A registration with no qualifying entry is still refused with `invalid_client_metadata`. Each dropped entry is logged as host and path with the Gemini binding segment masked (at most 10 per registration plus a count, log event `dcr_redirect_uris_dropped`). `https://oauth-redirect-test.googleusercontent.com` joins the two Gemini redirector hosts accepted since v0.120.0, so a Gemini custom MCP client that sends all three can register.
- **`/revoke` and `/introspect` read a bounded form body** ([#2207](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2207)): like `/token` and the consent POST, both refuse a form body over 16 KiB with `413` `invalid_request` before it is parsed and before the client is authenticated. A missing or empty `token` is `400` `invalid_request` (RFC 6749 JSON, `Cache-Control: no-store`); before this release it was a `422` validation error.

### Fixed
- **Repeated OAuth parameters on `/authorize`** ([#2208](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2208)): `response_type`, `client_id`, `redirect_uri`, `scope`, `state`, `code_challenge` and `code_challenge_method` may each be sent once in the query; a repeated one gets the `400` error page (`invalid_request`) on the GET and on the consent POST — before the client lookup, the redirect-URI check and the CSRF check — with no redirect and nothing stored. `resource` may still repeat (RFC 8707). Previously the last value silently won.
- **Erasure left an erased user's `details` in the revision log of memories they only edited** ([#2235](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2235)): the "no other details edit in between" check compared against every memory's edits instead of the row's own memory, so on a store with other edited memories the strip rarely matched. The check now correlates to the row's memory and also counts a details edit an earlier erasure already stripped, so two editors of one memory erased one after the other both have their value removed. See **Notes** for erasures that already ran.
- **Older migrations validated constraints under the ALTER lock** ([#2079](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2079)): `a97_resources_entity`, `b03_396_neural_edges_ws_ctx_not_null`, `d05_523_costgrade_source_paid_by`, `d07_495_cluster_label_phase` and `e01_546_cache_write_and_pricing_model` ran `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` in the upgrade transaction, which scans the table while the `ADD CONSTRAINT … NOT VALID` still holds its `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock. Each VALIDATE now runs in an autocommit block after the add commits (`SHARE UPDATE EXCLUSIVE` only), the steps before each block are re-runnable, and the `d07` downgrade keeps `cluster_labeling` ledger rows instead of failing on them. Every `VALIDATE` in `alembic/versions` is now checked by an AST test with no exceptions. Only installs upgrading from before those revisions see any difference.
- **Erasure scans of `memory_revisions`** ([#2115](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2115)): a new index on `actor_user_id` serves the erasure's pseudonymization UPDATE and session-provenance lookup, which had to walk a `memory_id`-led index or the table.
- **Redis client follows the event loop and is closed at shutdown** ([#1995](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1995)): the async Redis client is built per event loop (a uvicorn worker runs one, so production builds once) and a worker closes its Redis pool on shutdown — after the scheduler stops, before the database — bounded at 5 s and logged (`redis_connection_closed`, or `redis_close_failed`). This removes the `attached to a different loop` / `Event loop is closed` failures the test suite hit.

### Notes
- **Breaking for self-hosted production that kept a shipped placeholder.** After this upgrade the API container exits at import with a pydantic `ValidationError` naming the refused variables (`docker compose logs api`). `alembic upgrade head` and the admin CLIs are not stopped, so the single-server `deploy.sh` migrates, then aborts at the readiness wait **before traffic is switched** — the previous color keeps serving; this release's only migration adds an index, so the previous version runs on the migrated schema. Set the four values before deploying (`openssl rand -hex 32`; the single-server README has a loop that fills a fresh `.env.prod`). **Rotate only what is still a default**: replacing a private `AUDIT_HMAC_KEY` invalidates every known-device cookie (new-device notices) and breaks `verify_audit_chain` on earlier secret-store rows and open consent pages; a private `AUDIT_PSEUDO_SALT` breaks correlation between rows pseudonymized before and after; a private `API_KEY_SECRET` makes stored provider keys undecryptable. Details: `docs/deployment.md` → "Production secrets". `ENVIRONMENT=development` is not a workaround: it also drops the `Secure` flag on session cookies.
- **DCR clients:** compare the `redirect_uris` in the registration response with what you sent; an entry that was dropped is not accepted at `/authorize`. An entry that is not a redirect-URI pattern at all still fails the whole request with `422`.
- **Erasures that ran before this release** may have left the subject's `details` in `memory_revisions.prior` on memories the subject edited but did not author (the next details edit's prior). Those rows can be found by the subject's pseudonym in `actor_user_id`; re-processing them is tracked in [#2243](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2243). The live `memories.details` of such a memory, when the subject's edit was the last one, is not changed by erasure ([#2244](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2244)).
- **Migration `f10_2115_revisions_actor_idx`**: `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY idx_memory_revisions_actor ON memory_revisions (actor_user_id)` in an autocommit block (writes continue; an interrupted build is dropped and rebuilt on the next run). Downgrade drops it concurrently. Run `alembic upgrade head` online: this revision, and the older ones changed by #2079, read the database and do not run under `--sql`.
- **API:** `POST /api/v1/oauth/revoke` and `/introspect` document their form body, `400` and `413` RFC 6749 shapes in OpenAPI and no longer list `422`. No new routes. `GET`/`POST /authorize` gain the repeated-parameter `400` page. MCP tool count unchanged (72).
- **Logs:** new events `dcr_redirect_uris_dropped`, `oauth_authorize_get_rejected` / `oauth_authorize_post_rejected` with `reason=repeated_parameter`, `redis_connection_closed` at worker shutdown. Boot-time settings errors no longer echo the offending value (`hide_input_in_errors`); they name the variable.
- **Test suite** ([#2167](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2167), [#2180](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2180)): a repeat `setup_logger()` keeps the structlog processor list that cached loggers hold, so `capture_logs()` reaches them after an `api.main` import; the `DB_NULL_POOL` / `SCHEDULER_LEADER_ENABLED=false` test defaults apply to every test directory from the root `conftest.py`, with a fail-loud guard on import order. No production effect beyond #1995 above.
- No new environment variables. The four production secrets were already documented as required; this release enforces them.

## [v0.120.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.120.0) — 2026-10-21

Index and accounting correctness, plus Gemini DCR: a rebuilt resource point keeps the row's edited metadata and a re-pointed row leaves no stray chunks, the content-chunk pipeline closes its last reclaim, backfill and late-write gaps, k-NN seed edges are no longer built from text an edit replaced, a failed or cancelled analysis records its LLM spend, and an unscored rerank candidate is reported on the same scale as the scored ones; Gemini custom MCP clients can register through DCR.

### Added
- **Gemini custom MCP clients register through DCR** ([#2222](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2222)): Gemini's redirect URIs on `oauth-redirect.googleusercontent.com` and `oauth-redirect-sandbox.googleusercontent.com` (path `/r/user_bound_custom-mcp-<one segment>`, HTTPS, exact host) are accepted by `POST /register` as provider `gemini`, so Gemini can connect from the server URL alone. Other `googleusercontent.com` hosts and other paths are still refused.

### Changed
- **Unscored rerank candidates** ([#2084](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2084)): with a self-hosted reranker, a candidate the reranker could not score is now reported with a score at or below the lowest scored one — in recall `score`, the keyword-only confidence and co-activation — instead of its hybrid score on another scale. Order among unscored candidates is unchanged. The query cap on the raw `/v1/completions` path already shipped in v0.112.0 (#2097).
- **Stale embedding runs spend retry budget** ([#2095](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2095)): a `processing` row whose claim expired is reclaimed at most 3 times; then it ends `failed` with `embedding_error` `claim_expired: …` (log `embedding_budget_exhausted reason=claim_expired`) and shows as stalled, so an admin retry resets it. A worker restart in the middle of a run now spends 1 of the 3 retries.

### Fixed
- **Resource point rebuilds keep the row's edits** ([#2160](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2160)): a rebuilt (restore, Sleep reindex, embedding migration) or re-indexed resource point carries the row's tags, type, context_summary and location, so tag-, type- and geo-filtered recall finds the row again. Resource points written before this release get these fields the next time they are rebuilt or re-indexed.
- **Re-pointed rows leave no stray chunks** ([#2164](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2164)): when the resource indexer takes over a row by its natural key (for example a `remember(external_id=…)` row), the row's old content-chunk points and own-id point are deleted after the batch commits (best effort; the orphan vector sweep stays the backstop).
- **Content-chunk pipeline gaps** ([#2095](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2095)): turning `content_chunks_enabled` on also backfills rows whose embedding run was in flight; a Sleep chunk write that lands over a newer run still in flight re-queues the row instead of leaving pre-edit chunks.
- **k-NN seed edges after an edit** ([#2085](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2085)): seed edges are dropped when the memory's text changed between the run's success write and the edge commit (`knn_seed_skipped_source_edited`); the newer run seeds from the current text. Tag co-occurrence seeding still runs.
- **Spend of a failed or cancelled analysis** ([#2088](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2088)): an analysis that fails its cluster-labeling threshold or is cancelled writes a report with its LLM spend (tokens, per-model breakdown, `cost_actual_cents`), so cost aggregation counts it; the analysis views show the actual cost. A threshold failure no longer leaves the analysis stuck in `running`. Sleep health now grades Sleep reports only.

### Notes
- Sleep Reports lists now also show failed and cancelled analysis runs (status Failed / Cancelled, message `analysis_failed: …`), next to the successful analysis reports already listed there; a source filter is tracked in #2219. The spend of LLM calls still in flight when an analysis is cancelled cannot be recorded.
- No database migration, no new environment variables. MCP tool count unchanged. No OpenAPI change.

## [v0.119.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.119.0) — 2026-10-21

Auth and access hardening: the OAuth consent form carries a session-bound CSRF token and no longer lets form fields override the authorization request, OAuth form bodies are bounded, MCP looks a Bearer token up only in the store its shape names, and `list_revisions` no longer reveals a historical type the caller may not read.

### Changed
- **OAuth consent: query-only parameters and a CSRF token** ([#2199](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2199)): the consent page carries a hidden `csrf_token` bound to the browser session, and both *Approve* and *Deny* require it; a missing or wrong token gets the `400` error page and nothing is issued or stored. The approve passes Authlib the URL's query only; OAuth parameters in the form body (`client_id`, `redirect_uri`, `scope`, …) are ignored. The consent page is sent with `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none'`.
- **MCP Bearer routing** ([#1994](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1994)): a `kagura_` token is looked up only among API keys and any other token only among OAuth tokens, as on the REST API. An OAuth request no longer pays an API-key lookup; a miss in either store is the same `401`, and a failing lookup in one store no longer affects tokens of the other shape.

### Fixed
- **OAuth form bodies are bounded** ([#2200](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2200)): `POST /api/v1/oauth/token` and the consent `POST /api/v1/oauth/authorize` read at most 16 KiB of form body; a larger one gets RFC 6749 `invalid_request` with status `413` before it is parsed (nothing is spooled to disk).
- **`list_revisions` hides a denied historical type** ([#2169](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2169)): for a caller whose agent binding filters memory types, an `updated` event whose before or after version has a type the caller may not read lists `changed_fields: []`, the same rule as `reference(version=N)`. Event count, versions and paging are unchanged; unfiltered callers see no difference.
- **`DELETE /mcp` session lookup failure** ([#2074](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2074)): a session-store failure ends the session request with the same JSON-RPC `503` and `Retry-After` as GET and POST.

### Notes
- **Behaviour change for consent pages already open** when the server is upgraded: the page has no token, so submitting it gets the `400` page; reload the authorization URL. Rotating `AUDIT_HMAC_KEY` (which also keys the consent token) has the same effect on open consent pages.
- No database migration, no new environment variables. MCP tool count unchanged (72). No OpenAPI change.

## [v0.118.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.118.0) — 2026-10-20

Dependency refresh and the credential default-context follow-ups: the frontend moves to Next.js 16.4 with the open Dependabot advisories cleared, the Credentials page reads key defaults from one list and creates a key with its default in one call, the OAuth consent and token forms ignore file parts, and a bulk forget never outlasts one forget timeout.

### Changed
- **Member key responses carry the default context** ([#2188](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2188)): the member-credentials key list and create responses add `default_context_id`, `default_context_name` (`null` when none is set or the viewer cannot read the context) and `agent_id`; a key you mint for yourself accepts `default_context_id` (`404` `Context not found` and nothing created when unreadable, `422` for a public-bound key, `400` on the owner-provisioned path). The Credentials page drops its second key fetch, creates a key with its default in one call, keeps the create dialog open on an error, and hides the default-context control for agent-bound keys.
- **`forget(memory_ids=[...])` shares one time budget** ([#2191](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2191)): the ids of one call share the `forget` timeout (`MCP_TIMEOUT_FORGET`, default 15 s) instead of each getting their own; an id is not started when too little of the budget is left, and the rest come back as `error` / `not_attempted` to resend.

### Fixed
- **OAuth consent and token forms ignore file parts** ([#2190](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2190)): a multipart file part named like an OAuth field (`scope`, `state`, `code`, …) is dropped instead of reaching Authlib and ending in `server_error`; once a code is issued, a failure in a later step (consent default, new-app notice, loopback page) is logged and the client still gets its code. The generic authorize `server_error` redirect now sends a fixed `error_description` instead of the exception text.

### Notes
- **Dependencies** ([#2193](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2193)): `next` 16.2.6 → 16.4.2 (with `sharp` 0.35.5 and `postcss` 8.5.23), `eslint-config-next` 15 → 16, `vitest` 4.1.12, npm transitives refreshed within semver; backend `oauthlib` 3.3.1 → 4.0.0 (transitive, Google sign-in helper only). Two advisories without a usable patch were dismissed with reasons (`braces`, build tooling only; `postcss-selector-parser` 6.1.5 carries the backported fix).
- **API:** `MemberAPIKeyResponse` gains `default_context_id`, `default_context_name`, `agent_id`; `CreateAPIKeyRequest` (member route) accepts `default_context_id`. No new routes, no migration, no new environment variables. MCP tool count unchanged (72).

## [v0.117.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.117.0) — 2026-10-20

Client connection defaults and maintenance ergonomics: a default context can now live on the credential (API key or OAuth app), a loopback OAuth sign-in shows its callback URL instead of losing it, linked accounts can undo each other's sessions, and bulk forget takes a list of ids.

### Added
- **Default context bound to the credential** ([#2129](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2129)): a user API key and an OAuth app grant can carry an MCP default context — a context-scoped tool called without `context_id` runs in it, below `?context=` on the URL and above an agent-bound key's binding (`context_source: "credential"` on a refusal). Set it when creating a key or with `PATCH /api/v1/config/api-keys/{key_id}`, pick it on the OAuth consent page, or manage it under *Credentials & Integrations* (new *Default context* column and create-dialog picker, new *Connected apps* section) and with `GET /api/v1/me/oauth/grants`, `PUT`/`DELETE /api/v1/me/oauth/grants/{client_id}/default-context` (session-only). Setting one checks the context is readable now; every call is authorized again like an explicit id. Session cookies, connector keys, public-bound and agent-bound keys never carry one.
- **OAuth loopback interstitial** ([#2155](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2155)): after approving a client that redirects to `localhost` / `127.0.0.1` / `[::1]`, the server shows a *Return to <app>* page with the full callback URL and a *Continue* button instead of redirecting straight away, so a browser that cannot reach the listener (WSL2 NAT, SSH, another machine) can paste the URL into the client. Script-free (strict CSP), `no-store`, `no-referrer`; non-loopback clients, deny and error paths unchanged.
- **`forget(memory_ids=[...])`** ([#2127](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2127)): 1–50 distinct ids in one call, each handled exactly like `forget(memory_id=)` and committed on its own; the reply adds one `results` row per id (`deleted` / `skipped` / `error`) with `count`, `succeeded`, `skipped`, `failed`. The maintain skill now deletes picked items with one call.
- **Internal billing ownership re-check** ([#2181](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2181)): `GET /internal/workspaces/{workspace_id}/owner?sub=` (billing-service token) answers `is_owner` by strict equality with the workspace's owner pointer; `404` only when no workspace row exists, a deleted workspace answers `false`.

### Changed
- **`list_edges` hides weak edges by default** ([#2127](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2127)): `min_weight` defaults to 0.05 (the same floor as `explore`), the reply echoes the applied `min_weight`; pass `min_weight=0` for every edge.
- **Consent page** ([#2129](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2129)): an optional *Default context for this app* picker (grouped by workspace, "No default" first, "Keep the current default" when one is stored); the choice is saved only when a code is issued.

### Fixed
- **Linked identities address each other's write sessions** ([#2114](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2114)): `rollback_session` (session and window mode) and `changes_since` (`session_id`, `by_me`) count a session written under an account linked to yours as your own — the rule `list_revisions` uses for `by_you`. Linked callers share one window lock; an agent credential still reaches only its own agent's sessions; `session_not_found` stays uniform.

### Notes
- **Migration `f09_2129_cred_default_ctx`** (`api_keys.default_context_id` + two CHECK constraints + index; new table `oauth_grant_settings`). Downgrade drops them. No data step.
- **New routes:** `PATCH /api/v1/config/api-keys/{key_id}`; `GET /api/v1/me/oauth/grants`; `PUT` / `DELETE /api/v1/me/oauth/grants/{client_id}/default-context`; `GET /internal/workspaces/{workspace_id}/owner`. `APIKeyResponse` gains `default_context_id` / `default_context_name`; `APIKeyCreate` accepts `default_context_id`.
- **MCP:** `forget` gains `memory_ids`; `list_edges` gains `min_weight` in the reply; `context_source` adds `"credential"`; help texts for `context_id_required` and `by_me` updated. Tool count unchanged (72).
- **Behaviour change for loopback OAuth clients:** one extra page with a *Continue* button after consent (Claude Code, Cursor, `claude mcp add`, the remote OAuth verification script).
- No new environment variables.
- **Dependencies:** `aiohttp` 3.14.3 → 3.14.5 (transitive, via `aiobotocore` / `voyageai`) for the open Dependabot advisories on 3.14.3.

## [v0.116.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.116.0) — 2026-10-20

Search-result correctness: tag filters agree between `recall` and `list` and keep every requested tag, the recall LLM selector keeps the store's own facts and shows up in the cost dashboards, the embedding-model migration CLI records its switch time and never deadlocks with a rollback, and content chunks missing while a context was inactive are re-queued on their own.

### Added
- **Content chunk gaps close themselves** ([#2107](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2107)): where `CONTENT_CHUNKS_REQUIRE_PLAN` gates content chunks, a write that could not keep chunks (context inactive by plan or cap), a merge into such a context and an embedding switch stamp a gap on the context; a scheduler job (every 60 s, leader only, no setting) re-queues the memories without current chunks once the context is active again — after an upgrade, a larger cap, a sibling turning chunks off. Bounded per tick (5 contexts, 1,000 rows, at most 100 waiting per context and 200 per deployment). Logged as `content_chunks_gap_recorded` / `_requeued` / `_closed`.
- **Recall LLM selector spend in the cost dashboards** ([#2154](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2154)): selector calls appear as their own *By Source* row (`llm_selector`, also a `source` filter value of the cost aggregation API). A timed-out call counts as a call with unknown cost; selector rows now skip subscription pricing like the supersede judge.
- **`embedding_switched_at`** ([#2098](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2098)): every switch, flip and rollback of the migration CLI records its time on `context_search_configs`; `--rollback-to` re-queues from it by default and prints the boundary before asking. A context switched before this version falls back to the search config's last change with a printed warning; `--requeue-since` still overrides.

### Changed
- **Recall LLM selector prompt `selector-v3`** ([#2171](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2171)): the selector treats the store as the authority — a row is never dropped or cut because its fact contradicts the model's general knowledge — keeps every row a counting or listing query counts, and when two rows answer the same question differently prefers the newer one (each row now carries the UTC day it was last written; rank order is stated). `llm_call_log.call_metadata.prompt_revision` reads `selector-v3`. The row headers count against `LLM_SELECTOR_MAX_PROMPT_CHARS`.
- **Search settings save on confirm** ([#2159](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2159)): the content chunk and LLM result selection switches save as soon as their dialog is confirmed (one PUT carrying the stored values of the other fields); the save bar is for the numeric and reranker fields only, names what is unsaved, badges the card, and sits in the page flow instead of over it (the general settings bar too).
- **Orphan vector sweep** ([#2101](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2101)): a content chunk point of a live resource-document memory (written before the indexer took the row over) is an orphan (`chunk` column in the plan, `resource_chunk` reason); the sweep logs orphans by reason.
- **Embedding switch waits are bounded** ([#2143](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/pull/2143), [#2090](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2090)): the switch takes the point-writer lock for at most 30 s before changing routing and again before pruning; when the second wait expires the route stays switched and pruning is deferred to the orphan sweep (`prune_pending`, `context_embedding_prune_deferred`). Sleep's `undo_merge` and `rollback_sleep_run` resolve the embedding route under the shared lock.

### Fixed
- **`tags_match: "any"` with `tags_normalize` dropped requested tags past the 50-entry cap** ([#2096](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2096)): the widening is carried as one group per requested tag, so the cap counts requested tags (the first 50 distinct), not spellings.
- **`list` with `tags_match: "all"` + `tags_normalize` required every spelling** ([#2086](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2086)): each listed tag is now satisfied by any one of its spellings and same-fold tags with other numbers stay two requirements, as in `recall`.
- **`reference(version=N)` ignored the agent binding's per-memory type filter for version N** ([#2116](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2116)): the historical type is judged too, with the same not-found answer.
- **Metadata edits of a resource-document memory patched the wrong point** ([#2100](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2100)): `update_memory`, PATCH and `rollback_session` now merge tags / type / context summary / location into the row's own point (the indexer's), leave a point shared by another live row alone, and never write `importance` or `updated_at` onto an indexer point.
- **Embedding switch could deadlock with a session rollback** ([#2156](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2156)): the re-queue locks its rows `FOR NO KEY UPDATE NOWAIT`; a held row refuses the switch with routing unchanged and the CLI prints the exact retry (`--verify --switch --requeue-since <re-embed start>` after `--run`; `--rollback-to` again after a refused rollback), `--all` continues.
- **Session rollback lost the point-writer lock after a failed item** ([#2157](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2157)): the lock is taken once per call outside the item SAVEPOINTs, so an embedding switch cannot commit a new route while the rollback keeps writing under the old one.
- **Content chunk card shown as disabled on older backends** ([#2119](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2119)): the card needs both `content_chunks_enabled` and `content_chunks_can_change` from the server.

### Notes
- Migrations `f07_2098_embedding_switched_at` (one nullable `TIMESTAMPTZ` on `context_search_configs`, no backfill) and `f08_2107_chunk_gap` (nullable `content_chunks_gap_at`, `content_chunks_gap_cursor`, and a partial index `ix_context_search_configs_chunk_gap`; plain `CREATE INDEX` on a one-row-per-context table; no data step). Downgrades drop them.
- New scheduler job `reconcile_content_chunk_gaps` (60 s, leader only, no environment variable). With `CONTENT_CHUNKS_REQUIRE_PLAN=true` the leader stamps every context with the flag on once at its first tick after the upgrade (`content_chunks_gap_seeded`; marker row `marker:content_chunks_gap_seed` in `config_overrides`), so each gets one reconcile pass once active — Qdrant point reads, only memories without current chunks are re-queued. With the gate off nothing is stamped or recorded.
- No new environment variables. `LLM_SELECTOR_MAX_PROMPT_CHARS` now also counts the row headers.
- `--rollback-to` on a context switched before v0.116.0 warns and uses the search config's last change; pass `--requeue-since <switch time>` if the settings were edited after the switch.
- Cost dashboards: a timed-out selector call makes its bucket's cost `—` (sticky, like a pricing miss); tokens and call counts are still shown.

## [v0.115.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.115.0) — 2026-10-19

Cheaper reads for agents: `recall` can hand its page to a small model that keeps only the results that answer the query and cuts their content to verbatim passages (opt-in per context), `bootstrap` has a compact index shape for session start, and a deployment can set how many top results carry content by default.

### Added
- **Recall LLM selector** ([#2144](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2144)): with `update_search_config(llm_selector_enabled=true)`, an MCP `recall` sends the query and the page (summaries, plus the content the `full` rule already attached) to the deployment's judge model (`SLEEP_LLM_PROVIDER` / `SLEEP_LLM_MODEL`), which answers with row and segment numbers only. The server drops the rows marked irrelevant (`selector.dropped_memory_ids`, at most 20), cuts a kept row's `content` to the chosen stored passages joined with `" … "` and adds `spans: [{start, end}]`; nothing the model writes reaches a result. The envelope key `selector` is `{applied: true, model, kept, dropped, dropped_memory_ids}` or `{applied: false, reason}` with the unselected page, and is absent when the flag is off. Single-context MCP recalls only; REST recall, share keys, the agent bootstrap and `forget(query=)` never run it. One provider attempt under `LLM_SELECTOR_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (8), at most `LLM_SELECTOR_MAX_ROWS` (10) rows and `LLM_SELECTOR_MAX_PROMPT_CHARS` (24,000), `LLM_SELECTOR_MAX_CALLS_PER_DAY` (2,000) per workspace and UTC day; every failure returns the page unchanged. With `LLM_SELECTOR_REQUIRE_PLAN=true` the flag is the plan feature `llm_selector` (M and above) with `max_llm_selector_contexts` 0 / 1 / 5 / 20, earliest-enabled first; the search-config read reports `llm_selector_effective`, `_limit`, `_in_use`, `_disabled_reason` and `_can_change`. `guide(["recall"])` gains `recall.selector`.
- **LLM result selection in the Web UI** ([#2148](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2148)): the context's search settings get a switch for `llm_selector_enabled`, a confirmation that says where the text goes, the active / inactive state, and the plan, cap and permission reasons from the server.
- **`bootstrap(format="index")`** ([#2126](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2126)): pointer rows for session start. Pinned rows keep their full summary; guardrail, upcoming (`from` / `until`) and change rows carry an 80-character summary head with `summary_cut: true` when cut; there is no `instructions`. When `format` is omitted it is `index` for `max_chars` under 10,000 and `full` otherwise, so every call that was valid before keeps its shape byte for byte; `bootstrap` alone accepts `max_chars` down to 2,000. The `kagura-memory` plugin's session-start skill asks for `format="index", max_chars=3000` when the server lists `format`, and falls back to the plain call once on an older server.
- **`MCP_RECALL_DEFAULT_FULL`** ([#2140](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2140)): how many top results carry `content` when an MCP `recall` omits `full` (`0`–`100`, default 3). It is still a default, so only trusted, non-connector rows get content; an explicit `full` wins; REST stays at 0. A value out of range stops startup, like `MCP_RECALL_DEFAULT_VERBOSITY`.

### Changed
- **`recall` tool definition and guide** ([#2144](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2144)): the result keys name `spans?` and the envelope `selector?`; the `query` description is shorter to pay for them. `update_search_config` takes and returns `llm_selector_enabled`, and returns `llm_selector_effective`.
- **`recall_completed` gains `select_ms`** ([#2144](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2144)) when the selector ran; the recall audit row adds `selector_applied` / `selector_dropped_count`, and rows the selector dropped do not feed Hebbian learning.
- **Plan surfaces** ([#2144](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2144)): the admin plan change reports `llm_selector_deactivated` next to `content_chunks_deactivated`; the plan tier list, the admin quota breakdown and `/system/info` (`features.llm_selector_require_plan`) carry the new cap and switch; the context export includes `llm_selector_enabled`.

### Notes
- Migration `f06_2144_llm_selector`: `llm_selector_enabled BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false` (a constant default, metadata-only on PostgreSQL 11+) and a nullable `llm_selector_enabled_at` on `context_search_configs`. No backfill; no context is turned on by the upgrade.
- New optional environment variables, whose defaults keep v0.114.0 behaviour: `MCP_RECALL_DEFAULT_FULL` (3), `LLM_SELECTOR_REQUIRE_PLAN` (false), `LLM_SELECTOR_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, `LLM_SELECTOR_MAX_ROWS`, `LLM_SELECTOR_MAX_PROMPT_CHARS`, `LLM_SELECTOR_MAX_CALLS_PER_DAY`, and `PLAN_<KEY>_MAX_LLM_SELECTOR_CONTEXTS` (read only with the gate on). The selector needs the judge lane (`SLEEP_LLM_PROVIDER` / `SLEEP_LLM_MODEL`); without it an opted-in recall reports `reason: no_provider` and returns its page unchanged.
- Each selector call writes one `llm_call_log` row (`caller='recall'`, `call_metadata.stage='llm_selector'`); a timed-out call is recorded with zero tokens. The day counter is its own Redis key, separate from the judge's; with Redis unavailable no call is made. `recall_selector_failed_open` logs at WARNING, except `daily_cap`, which logs at INFO because `recall_selector_cap_reached` already warns once per workspace and day.
- Update the `kagura-memory` plugin together with the server to get the index-shaped session start; either side being older keeps the full shape.
- The MCP server still has 72 tools.

## [v0.114.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.114.0) — 2026-10-19

Fewer round trips for agents: the MCP URL can name a default context, `recall` returns the content of its top results and folds older versions of a fact under the newest, and clients without an MCP session can roll back their own writes by time window or name their session themselves.

### Added
- **Default context on the MCP URL** ([#2125](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2125)): `?context=<context_id>` names the connection's default context, and context-scoped tools called without `context_id` run in it (an explicit `context_id`, and recall's `context_ids`, win). The default is authorized exactly like an explicit id and never widens access; a default the caller cannot use is refused with the same error plus `context_source`. An agent-bound key without `?context=` defaults to its default or sole binding. `delete_context`, `update_context`, `update_search_config`, `merge_contexts` and the binding tools never take it. The server instructions name the default; `guide(["default-context"])` explains it. A credential-bound default (user API keys, OAuth) is #2129.
- **`rollback_session` by time window** ([#2121](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2121)): `rollback_session(context_id, since, until?, dry_run=true)` undoes the caller's own writes in a window of at most 7 days — the same planner, caps and continuation as the session mode — for stateless MCP clients that have no session id. The window selects only the caller's principal (user and the credential's agent); `changes_since(by_me=true)` previews it. New error `no_writes_in_window`.
- **Client session id on stateless requests** ([#2122](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2122)): a client can name its own session with `params._meta["ai.kagura/sessionId"]` on a `tools/call`, or the `Kagura-Session-Id` request header; same validation as the baggage id, bound to the caller's principal, precedence OTel baggage > `_meta` > header > `Mcp-Session-Id`. Write replies echo `session_id`, so `changes_since(session_id=)` and `rollback_session(session_id=)` work for those clients.
- **`recall` returns content for the top results** ([#2124](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2124)): `recall(full=N)` adds `content` (up to 1,500 characters; `content_truncated` / `content_next_offset` when cut) to the first N results, so an agent no longer needs a `reference()` round trip for them. On MCP the default is 3 and, when `full` is omitted, only trusted-tier non-connector rows receive content; an explicit `full` includes every row; `full=0` restores the summary-only shape. REST and internal callers default to 0. Under `max_chars`, content leaves the lowest-ranked rows first (`full_applied` reports how many kept it) before any row is cut. `bootstrap`, share-key recall and `forget(query=)` never include content.
- **`SUPERSEDE_SUGGEST_THRESHOLD_LLM`** ([#2123](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2123)): optional, default 0.75 (clamped 0.5–0.85). With `SUPERSEDE_VERIFY_MODE=llm` the supersede detection looks down to it, and a pair below 0.85 is stored as a candidate only with a judge verdict, so reworded updates of a fact reach the judge.

### Changed
- **Core tool descriptions trimmed** ([#2117](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2117)): the `tools/list` core profile is back under its 41,500-character budget with headroom; the detail moved to the `guide` topics. Behaviour and schemas are unchanged.
- **`context_id` optional in the schemas** ([#2125](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2125)): 30 context-scoped tools no longer list `context_id` under `required` because the URL default may supply it; a call that nothing supplies is still refused with `context_id_required`.
- **`recall` tool definition** ([#2124](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2124)): lists `full` and the content keys; the filter, rerank and confidence detail moved to `guide(["recall"])`.

### Fixed
- **Recall returned older versions of a fact next to the current one** ([#2123](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2123)): judge-verified older versions now fold under the newest (`folded: {count, memory_ids}` on that row), following the verdicts' direction, with the group's best score; `include_superseded=true` folds nothing. Folding only happens within one query's result pool.

### Notes
- Migration `f05_2121_rollback_window`: two nullable columns and a partial unique index on `memory_session_rollbacks`, and `idx_memory_revisions_actor_at` built `CONCURRENTLY` in an autocommit block (writes continue; re-run the upgrade if the build is interrupted; it cannot run offline with `alembic upgrade --sql`).
- New optional environment variable `SUPERSEDE_SUGGEST_THRESHOLD_LLM` (above). It sends more pairs to the judge: raise `SUPERSEDE_VERIFY_MAX_CALLS_PER_DAY` (default 200 per workspace) before a bulk ingest.
- Proxies in front of `/mcp` must pass the `Kagura-Session-Id` request header through; never set it statically (one value would put every conversation in one session).
- With `?guardrails=off&context=<id>` (the URL the Claude Code plugin recommends) the handshake performs one bounded DB read for the context name and `server/discover` becomes `cacheScope: private`.
- MCP `recall` now ships content for its top 3 results by default; clients that want the previous shape pass `full=0`. A deployment-level default is #2140.
- The MCP server still has 72 tools.

## [v0.113.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.113.0) — 2026-10-18

Memories get versions: every edit is recorded, `update_memory` can refuse a stale edit, past versions can be read, and one agent session's writes can be listed and rolled back. Content chunks become a plan feature with a per-plan cap and a toggle in the context's search settings, and recall loses a ~40 ms Qdrant stall.

### Added
- **Memory versions and revision log** ([#2043](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2043)): every memory has a `version` (returned by `remember`, `update_memory`, `reference`, `list` and full-verbosity `recall`). Each create, edit and forget is recorded in `memory_revisions`. `update_memory` and REST `PATCH` take an optional `expected_version`; a mismatch is refused before anything changes (MCP `version_conflict`, REST `409 RES-002` with `details.reason="version_conflict"`). Omitting it keeps last-writer-wins. Sleep, the resource indexer and embedding never bump the version.
- **`list_revisions` and `reference(version=N)`** ([#2043](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2043)): read a memory's change history and the memory as it stood at version N (MCP full profile; REST `GET /api/v1/memory/{id}/revisions` and `POST /reference` with `version`). Same permission as `reference`; a gap in the log is refused rather than guessed.
- **Session provenance and `rollback_session`** ([#2044](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2044)): every write records the writing agent (from the verified credential only) and session (OTel baggage, else the MCP session id); write tools return `session_id`. `changes_since(session_id=)` lists one session's writes. `rollback_session(context_id, session_id, dry_run=true)` (MCP) forgets what the session created, restores what it edited and un-forgets what it forgot, skipping with a cause any memory someone else changed since. A session is addressable only by its own principal, or by the context owner on a user credential. A rollback is final, is time-boxed per call and continues on the next call.
- **Content chunks as a plan feature** ([#2103](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2103)): feature `content_chunks` on M and above, with `max_content_chunk_contexts` S 0 / M 1 / L 5 / XL 20, enforced only when `CONTENT_CHUNKS_REQUIRE_PLAN=true` (default `false`, so self-hosted behaves as before). Enabling past the plan answers `403 FEAT-001`, past the cap `429 QUOTA-001` (`quota_type: content_chunk_contexts`). Over the cap or after a downgrade a stored `true` is inactive everywhere; the earliest-enabled contexts stay active and chunk points are kept. Search-config responses gain `content_chunks_effective`, `content_chunks_limit`, `content_chunks_in_use` and `content_chunks_disabled_reason`.
- **Content chunk toggle in search settings** ([#2104](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2104), [#2015](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2015)): the context's search settings page can turn chunks on and off, with confirmations that say what is sent to the embedding provider and reranker, the backfill count, and why the switch is disabled or inactive. The D9 eval numbers are on #2015.

### Changed
- **One effective content-chunk flag** ([#2080](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2080)): every reader of `content_chunks_enabled` goes through one policy (store support, plan, cap); `content_chunks_enabled` in responses still reports the stored value.
- **Sleep supersede judge releases its DB connection** ([#2070](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2070)): the judge call no longer holds a pooled connection; claim renewal runs every 15 s with a 5 s checkout bound; `embedding_claim_lost` carries `reason=requeued|stale`.
- **`update_memory` locks the row** ([#2043](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2043)) for the version check, including across its Qdrant payload sync.

### Fixed
- **~40 ms Qdrant REST stall on recall** ([#2112](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2112)): compressed, chunked REST responses were held by delayed ACK and Nagle at some response sizes (chunk-enabled recall and the rerank candidate query). The client now asks for uncompressed responses; `QDRANT_HTTP_COMPRESSION=true` turns compression back on for a Qdrant reached over a WAN.

### Notes
- Migrations `f02_2103_chunks_enabled_at`, `f03_2043_memory_versions` and `f04_2044_session_provenance` (metadata-only column adds, the `memory_revisions` and `memory_session_rollbacks` tables, and an index built `CONCURRENTLY`; re-run the upgrade if that build is interrupted).
- New optional environment variables: `CONTENT_CHUNKS_REQUIRE_PLAN` (default `false`) and `QDRANT_HTTP_COMPRESSION` (default `false`). Before turning `CONTENT_CHUNKS_REQUIRE_PLAN` on, run the pre-flight query in `docs/deployment.md` to list the contexts that would go inactive.
- The MCP server now has 72 tools (`list_revisions`, `rollback_session`).
- `memory_revisions` grows by one row per write; there is no retention cap yet. Account erasure scrubs revision details, session ids and actor ids.

## [v0.112.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.112.0) — 2026-10-18

Content chunks become operable: turning them on backfills existing memories, embedding-model migrations rebuild and purge them, Sleep re-embeds them only when the text changed and repairs a failed write, and their cost is counted. This release also fixes Sleep rollback and undo, which failed on every restore in v0.111.0.

### Added
- **Content chunk backfill on enable** ([#2015](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2015), PR 2b): turning `content_chunks_enabled` on now re-queues the context's existing memories with 1,500+ characters of content (not resource-indexer rows) in the same transaction, so the embedding sweep builds their chunks. Each one costs a summary embed and up to 16 chunk embeds. Search-config responses gain `content_chunks_can_change`, `content_chunks_supported`, `embedding_provider` and `content_chunks_backfill_pending` (memories still waiting).
- **`DEFAULT_CONTENT_CHUNKS_ENABLED`** ([#2015](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2015)): optional, default `false`. Sets `content_chunks_enabled` for **new** contexts only; existing contexts and a reset are never changed. `true` sends memory content, not only summaries, to the embedding provider. `true` with `KAGURA_VECTOR_BACKEND=lance` refuses to start. Shown as `search_defaults.content_chunks_enabled` in `/system/info`.
- **Embedding-model migration handles chunks** ([#2015](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2015), PR 2a): `--plan` estimates the chunk points, `--reembed` writes them into the target collection, `--verify` reports `CHUNKS_MISSING`, and when a switch follows the missing ones are rebuilt first; the switch is refused (exit 2) while any are left. Turning the flag off purges the context's chunks from every collection. Cross-context recall uses chunk vectors only for the listed contexts that have the flag on.

### Changed
- **Sleep re-embeds chunks only when the text changed** ([#2015](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2015)): a metadata-only change (importance, scope, tags) syncs the chunk payloads without embedding. Chunk embedding tokens now count in the Sleep run's totals.
- **`content_chunks_enabled` is refused on LanceDB** ([#2072](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2072)): REST answers `422 VAL-001` and MCP `invalid_search_config`. Turning it off is still allowed, and recall treats a stored `true` as off there.
- **`tags_match="all"` with `tags_normalize`** ([#2071](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2071)): each requested tag is its own group of spellings and the groups are ANDed. Tags that fold together but carry different numbers (`v0.11.0`, `v0.1.10`) stay separate, and multi-context recall requires every listed tag again.
- **Self-hosted rerank** ([#2062](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2062)): candidates the reranker could not score stay after the scored ones through the reinforce and graph-boost re-sorts. The query is cut to 1,000 characters on the Qwen3 paths and the Ollama `raw` prompt (`RERANK_PROMPT_FORMAT=raw` on `/v1/rerank` still sends it as is); keep an Ollama reranker's context at 4,096 tokens or more.
- **MCP per-request log lines at DEBUG** ([#2032](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2032)): `MCP request`, `MCP authenticated`, the session-id lines, `MCP handling`, the per-method lines and the stateless request line. At INFO a successful call no longer names its tool; `LOG_LEVEL=debug` brings the lines back. Session lifecycle, refusals, auth failures and errors keep their levels.
- **Sleep and analysis spend** ([#2060](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2060)): a Sleep phase that raises keeps the tokens it already spent; candidate-search embeddings count when a phase returns early; an unparseable judge answer counts its tokens but not a call. **`calls` now counts successful calls only** (`sleep_report_llm_usage.calls`, `llm_calls_made`, analysis `llm_calls_used`), so it reads lower than before for the same traffic. Cache-write tokens are recorded and priced; a model with cache writes but no `cache_write_tokens` price shows its cost as unknown.
- **Unsearchable-backlog warning by cause** ([#2015](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2015)): the periodic warning splits failed rows into `capped`, `configuration` and `other`, and a failed row's `embedding_error` starts with the error code.

### Fixed
- **Sleep rollback and undo work again** ([#2065](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2065)): in v0.111.0 every archive or merge restore raised `MissingGreenlet`. `rollback_sleep_run` then committed the restored row without a vector (and `embedding_status` stayed `success`, so nothing rebuilt it), and the REST per-merge undo returned 500. Each run-level undo now runs in its own savepoint, and a restored row whose vector cannot be written is re-queued (`points_requeued`). Resource-indexer rows are archived, merged and re-weighted through their own point, and the orphan sweep removes their stray label points. See Notes for v0.111.0 installs.
- **Chunk writes replace in place and repair themselves** ([#2061](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2061)): chunks are upserted first and the tail trimmed after, so a failed Sleep write keeps the old chunks and re-queues the memory (`embedding_requeued`; rollback reports `chunks_requeued`). A Sleep write overtaken by a newer run, or an embedding run that lost its claim after writing, re-queues the row. The 30-second per-process chunk-flag cache is gone, so every metadata change reads the current flag.
- **Embedding-model switch re-queues Sleep-restored memories** ([#2063](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2063)): a memory restored by an undo or rollback after `--verify` is re-queued by `--switch` and `--rollback-to`.
- **`--rollback-to` without `--requeue-since`** (refs [#1525](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1525)): failed with an asyncpg `DataError` (timezone-aware boundary on naive columns). The boundary is now normalised to UTC. Pass the switch time explicitly if the context's search settings changed after the switch.
- **Supersede candidates are never written over a newer decision** ([#2069](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2069)): every path re-reads the row before writing, and the write applies only while the row is unchanged, so a dismissal, accept, edit or delete committed meanwhile wins (`supersede_candidate_skipped_row_changed`).
- **Constraint validation outside the ALTER lock** ([#2073](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2073)): migrations `e74`, `e84`, `e98` and `f00` validate their CHECK in an autocommit block, so ledger writes no longer wait while existing rows are checked. This only affects installs upgrading from before those revisions.

### Notes
- No new migration. New optional environment variable: `DEFAULT_CONTENT_CHUNKS_ENABLED`.
- **If you ran a Sleep rollback or undo on v0.111.0**: look for `rollback_action_failed` with `exc_type=MissingGreenlet`, or Sleep reports with `status='failed'` whose error starts with `Partial rollback`. Re-queue the restored memories with `UPDATE memories SET embedding_status='pending' WHERE id IN (...) AND deleted_at IS NULL`; the embedding sweep rebuilds their vectors.
- Turning content chunks on now spends embeddings for the existing long memories; the remaining count is `content_chunks_backfill_pending`.
- Expect lower `calls` in the cost dashboards from this release.
- The UI toggle for content chunks (#2015 PR 2c) moves to v0.113.0, after the eval numbers.

## [v0.111.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.111.0) — 2026-10-18

Hardening after v0.110.0: deploys migrate before the new code starts, content chunk writes are retried and can no longer be overwritten by a stale embedding run, Sleep keeps a memory's BM25 vector, supersede verdicts go stale by one rule, and `GET /mcp` stops a client reconnect loop.

### Changed
- **`GET /mcp` answers `405`** ([#2057](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2057)): the server sends no server-initiated messages, so the optional GET stream now gets `405 Method Not Allowed` with `Allow: POST, DELETE`, as the MCP Streamable HTTP spec allows. It used to answer `200 text/event-stream` and close at once, and MCP SDK clients reconnected in a tight loop (auth and a session lookup per reconnect, plus several log lines). A GET naming an ended, foreign or never-issued session still gets `404`, and a GET no longer opens a session. Every other `405` on `/mcp` now also lists `POST, DELETE`.
- **`deploy.sh` migrates before it starts the new color** ([#2048](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2048)): `alembic upgrade head` runs as a one-off `compose run --rm --no-deps` container before the inactive color starts, so its startup tasks never meet a schema that is one migration behind. A failed migration stops the deploy before anything is switched. In registry mode the script checks that the pulled image is the one compose will run. `--rollback` still runs no migrations.

### Fixed
- **Sleep reindex and undo keep the BM25 sparse vector** ([#2053](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2053)): both paths rewrote a memory's point without its sparse vector, so after a Sleep run the memory dropped out of keyword (BM25) recall. They now build the point the same way the embedding pipeline does. Resource-indexer rows are rebuilt through the resource indexer.
- **Failed content chunk writes are retried** ([#2049](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2049)): with `content_chunks_enabled` on, a transient failure (most often the self-hosted reachability probe) left a memory stamped `success` with no chunk vectors. The embedding pipeline now marks the row failed so the sweep retries it, and a successful self-hosted probe is cached per process for 60 seconds instead of running on almost every embed.
- **A stale embedding run cannot overwrite a newer one** ([#2041](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2041)): the chunk writer checks, before it deletes and again before it writes, that the memory was not edited, deleted or claimed by a newer run meanwhile. The run's success and failure updates only apply to its own claim, and a live run renews its claim so a slow run is not taken over. Every summary-only Qdrant scroll now uses one shared filter that excludes chunk points, and a test fails on a new scroll that does not.
- **`tags_match="all"` with `tags_normalize`** ([#2058](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2058)): the widened filter ORed every spelling of every requested tag together, so a memory with only one of two requested tags matched. Each requested tag is now its own group (any of its spellings), and the groups are ANDed.
- **Supersede verdicts go stale by one rule** ([#2059](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2059)): with `SUPERSEDE_VERIFY_MODE=llm`, a `parallel` suggestion shown after the older memory was edited could not be dismissed, and an edit during the judge call could keep `verified: true` on text the judge never saw. A verdict now counts only while both memories' texts match what the judge saw; a run whose memory was edited during the judge call writes nothing; and a re-embed that finds no near neighbour clears a live candidate (dismissals are never cleared).
- **Failed Sleep LLM calls count their tokens** ([#2038](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2038)): tokens a failed call still spent now reach the Sleep report totals and count against `max_llm_calls`; the analysis fallback chain includes a failed attempt's spend in its cost.

### Notes
- No migration, no new environment variables.
- Self-hosted operators using `terraform/single-server/scripts/deploy.sh`: the migration step now runs before the new color starts (see "Migration discipline" in `terraform/single-server/README.md`).
- Content chunk defaults and backfill ([#2015](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2015)) move to v0.112.0.

## [v0.110.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.110.0) — 2026-10-17

Retrieval that can see content and a reranker that cannot quietly make recall worse: opt-in content chunk vectors for dense retrieval, the Qwen3-Reranker prompt template on the vLLM and Ollama paths with a fail-safe for scores that carry no signal, the supersede judge's calls in the cost ledger, and Japanese tag variants folded together.

### Added
- **Opt-in content chunk vectors** ([#2015](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2015), PR 1 of 2): with the new per-context search setting `content_chunks_enabled` (default `false`), dense retrieval can see a memory's `content`, not only its summary.
  - How it works: a memory whose `content` is at least 1,500 characters also gets up to 16 chunk vectors (1,200 characters each, overlapping by 200) in the same Qdrant collection. A recall hit on a chunk counts for its memory, and the reranker sees the matched passage.
  - Data handling: chunks are removed with their memory on every delete path. Turning the setting off removes the context's chunks. Editing a memory rebuilds its chunks.
  - Privacy: turning it on sends memory **content** to the embedding provider, and to the rerank provider when reranking is on. Only the context owner or a workspace owner or admin can change the setting.
  - Defaults and backfill follow in v0.111.0, after eval numbers.
- **Supersede judge calls in the cost ledger** ([#2019](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2019)): every call by the #2016 judge that spends tokens now writes a `llm_call_log` row with tokens, the cost at write time, `paid_by`, the verdict and the outcome. Verdict and token tallies survive log rotation. The admin and workspace cost dashboards show cost per source. Sleep keeps its per-run totals in `sleep_reports`.
- **Qwen3-Reranker prompt template** ([#2020](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2020)): `VLLMReranker` (`RERANK_BASE_URL`) now formats the query and documents with the template the model was trained on. Without it, self-hosted rerank on the eval slice scored recall@5 0.101 against 0.839 with no rerank; with it, 0.874.
  - New settings: `RERANK_PROMPT_FORMAT=auto|qwen3|raw` (default `auto`) and `RERANK_INSTRUCTION`.
  - Prompt-injection fix: Qwen3 control tokens in memory text are neutralised, so a stored memory can no longer write its own "yes" verdict.
  - At startup each API process runs one calibration check with a relevant and an irrelevant document, and logs `rerank_model_uncalibrated` if they don't separate.
- **Japanese tag variants fold together** ([#2028](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2028)): `normalize_tag` now maps hiragana to katakana (`ろぐ` = `ログ`) and drops one word-final `ー` from a katakana word of 3 or more characters (`サーバー` = `サーバ`). This applies to `tags_normalize` on write, to recall filters, and to the `tag_near_duplicate` lint. Kanji and their readings, okurigana and a long-vowel mark inside a word stay apart.

### Changed
- **Container log rotation is configurable** ([#2025](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2025)): `API_LOG_MAX_SIZE` / `API_LOG_MAX_FILE` set the API colors' cap, and `LOG_MAX_SIZE` / `LOG_MAX_FILE` set every other service's. The defaults stay 50m × 3. `deploy.sh` checks the values before it touches a container. The per-request `MCP auth success` lines are now DEBUG; auth failures still log at WARNING.
- **One recall row shape for recall and agent bootstrap** ([#2013](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2013)): `get_agent_bootstrap` (MCP and REST) builds its recall rows with the `recall` projection (`verbosity="full"`). See Migration.
- **`knn_seed_skip_no_consumer` is visible at INFO** ([#2023](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2023)): it logs once per embedder per process at INFO, then at DEBUG. `docs/deployment.md` lists every supersede and k-NN seeding log event and its level.

### Fixed
- **Ollama rerank no longer zeroes every score** ([#2033](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2033)): the Ollama path asked Qwen3 rerankers for a number with a generic prompt and got `0` for almost every real memory, which overwrote every `hybrid_score`. It now scores Qwen3 rerankers by the yes/no probability through `/api/generate` (needs Ollama 0.12.11 or later). A guard shared by every provider fails open when scores are all zero or mostly unscored, and keeps the hybrid order when the scores are identical.
- **`supersede_candidate` stores SQL NULL** ([#2022](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2022)): "no candidate" was stored as JSON `null`, so `WHERE supersede_candidate IS NULL` counted almost nothing. Migration `e99_2022_supersede_cand_null` rewrites existing rows in batches.
- **A supersede candidate from a connector row no longer reaches a trusted recall** ([#2013](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2013)): under `trust_tier=trusted`, a connector-sourced memory could appear through another row's `supersede_candidate`.
- **`dedupe="off"` is documented as permanent** ([#2024](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2024)): the guide and the tool description now say up front that `off` permanently opts that memory out of supersede suggestions.

### Migration
- **Bootstrap recall rows** (`get_agent_bootstrap` MCP tool, `POST /api/v1/agents/{id}/bootstrap`), [#2013](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2013):
  - An empty `context_summary`, `tags`, `superseded_by` or `contradicts` is now **absent** instead of `null` / `[]`.
  - `score` is rounded to 4 decimals.
  - Rows never carry `supersede_candidate`.
  - Read these keys with a default, e.g. `row.get("tags", [])`.
- **Self-hosted rerank** ([#2020](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2020), [#2033](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2033)):
  - If your vLLM server already applies a template with `--chat-template`, set `RERANK_PROMPT_FORMAT=raw`. Otherwise the template is applied twice.
  - The Ollama path's `qwen3` scoring needs Ollama 0.12.11 or later. The `0.6B` Qwen3-Reranker GGUF does not work on Ollama; use the 8B model or `RERANK_BASE_URL`.

### Notes
- Migrations, in order:
  1. `e99_2022_supersede_cand_null`: data only, batched and resumable.
  2. `f00_2019_llm_log_supersede`: widens the `llm_call_log` caller CHECK. Before v0.112.0, ledger writes wait while existing rows are checked ([#2073](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2073)).
  3. `f01_2015_content_chunks`: adds a NOT NULL bool column with a server default.
- New optional environment variables: `RERANK_PROMPT_FORMAT`, `RERANK_INSTRUCTION`, `API_LOG_MAX_SIZE`, `API_LOG_MAX_FILE`, `LOG_MAX_SIZE`, `LOG_MAX_FILE`.
- New log rotation values take effect only when a container is recreated. A reboot recreates every service whose configuration changed.
- With content chunks off (the default), recall is unchanged. At startup, each process adds the `chunk_of` payload index to existing memory collections in the background.

## [v0.109.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.109.0) — 2026-10-17

Supersede suggestions a client can trust: a deployment can put each cosine candidate to the Sleep judge before it surfaces, and the suggestion detection has its own switch instead of riding on the k-NN seed flag.

### Added
- **Verified supersede suggestions** ([#2016](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2016)): similarity alone proposed a supersede for every true update and for every sibling-entity negative on the eval slice (two depots with different opening hours read as one updated fact). With `SUPERSEDE_VERIFY_MODE=llm` the Sleep judge (`SLEEP_LLM_PROVIDER` / `SLEEP_LLM_MODEL`, same credential lane as Sleep) is asked once per pair whether the new memory replaces the old one: a `parallel` verdict is stored and never surfaced, a `supersedes` verdict at or above `SUPERSEDE_VERIFY_MIN_CONFIDENCE` (default 0.7) surfaces the candidate with `verified: true` on `recall` / `reference`, and `unclear`, a judge that is off (the default), over `SUPERSEDE_VERIFY_MAX_CALLS_PER_DAY` (default 200 per workspace) or failing leaves the candidate as before. An unmoved pair is never re-judged on a re-embed; the judge never creates the edge. The `maintain` skill reads the flag.

### Fixed
- **Supersede suggestions have their own switch** ([#2010](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2010)): `KNN_SEED_ENABLED=false` switched off the suggestion detection too, silently. `SUPERSEDE_SUGGEST_ENABLED` (default `true`) now gates the post-embed detection alone; seed edges off still stores candidates, suggestions off still seeds edges, both off skips the neighbour query, and seeding on without a calibration row no longer runs a query nothing reads. One INFO line per off-transition.

### Behaviour changes
- `supersede_candidate` on MCP `recall` / `reference` omits `detected_at` when it has no value (it was `null`), per the "absent, never null" rule; `verified` is a key only when `true`.
- Deployments with `KNN_SEED_ENABLED=false` now get supersede suggestions (`SUPERSEDE_SUGGEST_ENABLED=false` turns them off again).

### Migration
- None. New env switches are optional: `SUPERSEDE_SUGGEST_ENABLED`, `SUPERSEDE_VERIFY_MODE`, `SUPERSEDE_VERIFY_MIN_CONFIDENCE`, `SUPERSEDE_VERIFY_MAX_CALLS_PER_DAY` (production values belong to the devops repo).

## [v0.108.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.108.0) — 2026-10-16

The MCP session-start path points agents at `bootstrap`, `recall` answers compactly by default, `remember` stops refusing a long summary or mislabelling a too-large memory, supersede suggestions fire on uncalibrated embedders, agent denies on `list` / `changes_since` are uniform again, and a deployment can opt in to pushing a user's guardrails at connect.

### Added
- **Opt-in last-used guardrail digest at connect** ([#2000](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2000)): with `MCP_GUARDRAIL_DIGEST_LAST_USED_FALLBACK=true` (off by default), a user credential with neither `?guardrails=` nor an agent binding receives in the `initialize` / `server/discover` instructions the digest of the context its own most recent tool call touched (one narrow `usage_stats` read in the last 30 days; a fresh account's sole context). A connector's shared key never gets it; the default keeps the plain-URL handshake static, DB-free and publicly cacheable as the Directory scope describes.
- **`recall` `verbosity`** ([#2002](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2002)): `"compact"` (the default) returns per hit `memory_id`, the first 160 characters of `summary` (`summary_cut: true` when shortened), `type`, `importance`, `score`, `updated_at` and the actionable annotations (`superseded_by`, `contradicts`, `supersede_candidate`), with `confidence` as the first key; `"full"` is the previous shape. A k=5 reply is about half the size.
- **Tolerant `remember`** ([#2003](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2003)): a summary over 500 characters is cut at a sentence end (the full text is kept at the head of `content`) and the reply says `summary_truncated: true` with `summary_original_length`; the `summary_long` lint is muted on a cut. A memory over 1 MB answers `payload_too_large` with `limit`, `actual` and a help line instead of `quota_exceeded`; `remember_batch` items behave the same, and `missing_fields` carries the same help.

### Changed
- **Server instructions and Quick Reference start at `bootstrap`** ([#2001](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2001)): the base text names `bootstrap(context_id)` as the session-start call and `changes_since` for "what changed, when" beside `remember` / `recall`; the Quick Reference's Session Start is tool-neutral (it is also `get_agent_bootstrap`'s instructions). Core-profile tool text that pointed at `create_edge` / `recall_nearby` now says those sit outside the core profile and that `describe_tools([...])` has their schema; the `guide` topics carry the same qualifier and a test keeps it so.

### Fixed
- **Supersede suggestions without a `knn_seed` row** ([#2006](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2006)): the detection no longer depends on the k-NN seed calibration row; only seed-edge creation is skipped without one (0/100 → 100/100 suggestions on an uncalibrated embedder). An uncalibrated embedder past the D3 gate logs `knn_seed_uncalibrated_embedder` once per process.
