- **Uniform agent denies on `list` / `changes_since`** ([#2007](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/2007)): both (and `recall_upcoming` / `recall_nearby`) are in the memory-access-event vocabulary (migration `e98_2007`), so an unbound agent gets `context_not_found` in enforce mode and a `would_deny` event in shadow mode instead of `validation_error`; a future vocabulary gap degrades to an error log, never a different API response.

### Behaviour changes
- `recall` is compact by default: clients that read `tags`, `context_summary`, `scope` or `created_at` from a result must pass `verbosity="full"` (or read one hit with `reference`).
- `remember` / `remember_batch` store a long summary instead of refusing it; `payload_too_large` is a new MCP error code (REST keeps 429 `QUOTA-001`).

### Migration
- `alembic upgrade head` (e98_2007: widens the `valid_mae_operation` CHECK; zero-downtime).
- Optional: `MCP_GUARDRAIL_DIGEST_LAST_USED_FALLBACK=true` to push a user's last-used guardrails at connect (production values belong to the devops repo).

## [v0.107.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.107.0) — 2026-10-16

Every recall now tells which stage its time went to, a database outage during a REST credential lookup answers 503 instead of 401, the files left in contexts deleted before v0.101.0 can be cleared so the upload quota and the capacity lock count the same bytes, and the test suite no longer stalls on a pooled connection reused across event loops.

### Added
- **Per-stage timings on `recall_completed`** ([#1989](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1989)): every completed recall logs one `recall_completed` line (also when the page is empty) with integer milliseconds per stage — `duration_ms`, `embed_ms` + `embed_cache_hit`, `qdrant_ms`, `fulltext_ms`, `rerank_ms` (only when a provider scored, or the rerank failed open), `hydrate_ms`, `finalize_ms`, `commit_ms`, `post_commit_ms` (the #1985 writes that still delay the response) and `other_ms` (the unmeasured remainder, so the stages add up to `duration_ms`) — plus `candidates`, `results`, `k`, `context_count` and `degraded`. `docs/deployment.md` "Recall stage timings" explains each field and gives a `jq` recipe for p50 / p95 per stage from the live color's JSON log.
- **Backfill for files left in contexts deleted before v0.101.0** ([#1966](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1966)): until #1960 deleting a context left its files live — hidden from every listing but still counted by the upload quota, while the capacity lock and the downgrade eligibility never counted them, so a workspace could downgrade to Free and then have every upload refused with no lock to explain it. `python -m src.cli.backfill_deleted_context_files` (plan by default; `--apply`, `--yes`, `--workspace <id>`) gives those files the deletion their context had, one context per transaction, and rewrites the workspace's Redis counter from the shared definition after each commit. A context deleted more than the 6-day file restore window ago is listed as `past window` and skipped unless `--include-past-window` is given: its files cannot be restored and the next nightly file GC removes their objects for good.
- **Test-process switches for the database pool and the scheduler** ([#1988](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1988)): `DB_NULL_POOL=true` gives both SQLAlchemy engines a `NullPool` (one fresh connection per checkout; `DB_POOL_*` are validated but unread) and `SCHEDULER_LEADER_ENABLED=false` keeps a process from starting the scheduler leader. Both are for test processes that run several event loops against one engine; the defaults are unchanged and each logs a warning when switched (`database_null_pool_enabled`, `scheduler_leader_disabled`).

### Changed
- **One storage figure** ([#1966](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1966)): the Redis upload counter reseeds from the same statement the capacity lock and the downgrade eligibility use (`services/storage_counts.py`: committed files in live contexts or workspace-scoped), so the three gates can no longer disagree. `workspace_storage_usage` stays a delta-maintained approximation of that figure.
- **`recall_completed` is logged last** ([#1989](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1989)): after the audit row and the confidence computation rather than before them, and on the empty-result exit too; `user_id` and `results` are unchanged, fields are only added.

### Fixed
- **A database outage during a REST credential lookup answered `401`** ([#1981](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1981)): the OAuth Bearer lookup swallowed every `SQLAlchemyError` and read a pool timeout as an invalid token, so clients discarded their token and re-authorized instead of retrying. The lookup now propagates and the request gets `503` `DB-002` with `details.reason` (`pool_exhausted`, `database_error`, `connection_refused`) and `Retry-After`, like any other request; the MCP transport keeps answering `503`. The standalone API-key verifier releases its session at once when a lookup fails.
- **`backend-integration` stalled for its whole timeout in a `TestClient` lifespan** ([#1988](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1988)): pytest-asyncio's session loop and each lifespan's portal loop shared the process-global engine, and a pooled asyncpg connection reused across loops was terminated from the wrong loop (`attached to a different loop`). The integration suite runs with `DB_NULL_POOL=true` and `SCHEDULER_LEADER_ENABLED=false`, the scheduler leader is pinned against Postgres by its own test, and `backend-unit` runs with a 300 s per-test timeout (a guard keeps every backend pytest job in CI on `--timeout`).

### Notes
- No migration. New environment variables, both for test processes only with unchanged defaults: `DB_NULL_POOL` and `SCHEDULER_LEADER_ENABLED` (leave the leader on in every deployment; with it off everywhere, no process runs the cron jobs). Operator action after deploying: run `python -m src.cli.backfill_deleted_context_files` once (plan first; `--include-past-window` is a permanent deletion, see `docs/deployment.md`). New log events: `recall_completed` fields, `storage_quota_counter_reseeded` / `storage_quota_counter_reseed_failed`, `deleted_context_files_backfilled` / `deleted_context_files_backfill_skipped`, `database_null_pool_enabled`, `scheduler_leader_disabled`; `oauth_bearer_token_lookup_failed` is gone (the failure is logged by `database_error` with `reason` on REST). Follow-ups (backlog): #1994 (MCP tries the API-key store before the OAuth store for every token), #1995 (the process-global Redis client has the same cross-loop shape).

## [v0.106.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.106.0) — 2026-10-15

The dense vectors in Qdrant can be stored as int8 with the fp32 originals on disk, cutting the RAM a single-server deployment needs per memory to about a quarter; existing collections are converted in place at startup.

### Added
- **Dense vector quantization and on-disk storage** ([#1990](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1990)): `QDRANT_DENSE_QUANTIZATION=int8` keeps a scalar-quantized (1 byte per dimension) copy of each memory's dense vector in RAM for the search and rescores the candidates against the fp32 originals (oversampling 2.0), so the ranking stays within Qdrant's documented <1% recall loss; `QDRANT_DENSE_ON_DISK=true` stores those originals as memory-mapped files instead of RAM. Both default to the historical fp32-in-RAM layout. A new collection is created with the configured layout; right after startup the API reconciles every existing `kagura_memories*` collection in a background task, one at a time and one worker at a time (Postgres advisory lock), sending one `update_collection` only where the layout differs (`collection_dense_config_updated`) — nothing is re-embedded or re-created, in either direction. `docs/deployment.md` has the RAM formula (`points × dimensions × 4 B` fp32, `× 1 B` int8), the conversion's transient RAM and disk peak while the optimizer rebuilds segments, and the rollback; `backend/scripts/check_qdrant_collections.py` prints each collection's status and layout, `backend/scripts/bench_dense_quantization.py` measures the effect on a throwaway collection (on a 20,000 × 2560 clustered corpus int8 with rescore returned the fp32 top-10 exactly, at about +3 ms p50).

### Notes
- No migration. New environment variables, both optional with unchanged defaults: `QDRANT_DENSE_QUANTIZATION` (`none` | `int8`) and `QDRANT_DENSE_ON_DISK`. Operators turning int8 on for an existing deployment should do so while the host still has free RAM and in a maintenance window: until the optimizer swaps the rebuilt segments, old and new coexist. The LanceDB backend ignores both settings (`dense_vector_config_ignored`). New log events: `collection_dense_config_updated`, `collection_dense_config_skipped` (a collection quantized outside the service is left alone), `collection_dense_config_apply_failed`, `dense_vector_config_reconciled`.

## [v0.105.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.105.0) — 2026-10-15

Concurrent recalls no longer deadlock: the access statistics and the Hebbian learning a recall records are written after the read commits, in id-ordered statements that never wait on another transaction's row.

### Fixed
- **Concurrent recalls deadlocked on their own statistics** ([#1985](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1985)): two recalls whose pages overlapped bumped `access_count` row by row in ranking order inside the read transaction, then upserted `neural_memory_edges` edge by edge, so each could hold a row the other wanted; Postgres aborted one with `DeadlockDetectedError` (`503 DB-002`), and at concurrency 32 every request timed out. A recall now returns its page from a committed read, then records the page's access statistics (and any promotion to persistent) in one statement that locks rows in `id` order through an ordered `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` subquery, and runs the Hebbian pass in its own transaction under a per-user advisory lock (`hebbian:<user_id>`), skipping the pass when another transaction holds it. Sleep and the neural batch tasks take the same lock for their run, and promote through the same id-ordered statement. A failure in either post-commit step is logged (`access_stats_update_failed`, `hebbian_update_failed`) and never fails the recall.

### Changed
- **`503 DB-002` reason `deadlock`** ([#1985](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1985)): a transaction Postgres aborted to break a lock cycle (SQLSTATE `40P01`) is reported with `details.reason: "deadlock"` (REST) and `cause: service_unavailable` (MCP); code, status, message and `Retry-After: 5` are unchanged. A retry normally succeeds.
- **`access_count` is a lower bound** ([#1985](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1985)): a statistics write skips a row another transaction holds at that moment instead of waiting, so a bump can be lost under contention. Promotion to persistent is decided from the row as the statistics write left it, so concurrent recalls by different clients add up. Sleep report counters (`rule_promoted`, `llm_promoted`, `total_promoted`) count rows actually promoted rather than attempts.

### Notes
- No migration, no new environment variables, no operator action. New log events: `hebbian_update_skipped_busy` (a learning pass skipped because another transaction, or that user's Sleep run, holds the graph lock) and `access_stats_update_failed`. Follow-ups (backlog): Sleep's archive tombstones and the legacy delete still write rows one at a time; the statistics write could move to a background task.

## [v0.104.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.104.0) — 2026-10-15

Scaling the API past one process: the PostgreSQL pool is configurable and tells pool exhaustion apart from an outage, the single-server deployment can run several uvicorn workers with a right-sized Postgres, and the background scheduler runs on exactly one of them.

### Added
- **Configurable PostgreSQL connection pool** ([#1980](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1980)): `DB_POOL_SIZE` / `DB_MAX_OVERFLOW` / `DB_POOL_TIMEOUT` size both SQLAlchemy engines (the async application engine and the sync OAuth2 engine); the defaults stay 5 / 10 / 30. A production bench had exhausted the fixed 15-connection pool at concurrency 8. The API logs `database_pool_below_fanout` when the configured pool is smaller than the 15 connections the analysis labeler assumes.
- **Several API workers per container and a Postgres `max_connections` knob** ([#1980](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1980)): the single-server compose files start the API with `--workers ${UVICORN_WORKERS:-1}` and Postgres with `max_connections=${POSTGRES_MAX_CONNECTIONS:-100}`; both default to today's values. `docs/deployment.md` "Multiple API workers and Postgres connections" has the sizing rule (`UVICORN_WORKERS × 2 × (DB_POOL_SIZE + DB_MAX_OVERFLOW)` per color, doubled during a blue/green switch), the rollout order and the known limits with several workers.
- **One scheduler per deployment** ([#1980](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1980)): every API process used to start its own APScheduler, so several workers would have run the cron jobs several times. The process that holds the Postgres advisory lock `apscheduler_leader` now runs the scheduler (`scheduler_leader_acquired`); the others stand by (`scheduler_leader_standby`) and take over within `SCHEDULER_LEADER_RETRY_SECONDS` (default 60) when it exits. Startup no longer waits on Postgres for the scheduler.

### Changed
- **`503 DB-002` carries `details.reason`** ([#1980](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1980)): `pool_exhausted` (the pool checkout timed out), `connection_refused` (the database did not accept the connection) or `database_error` (any other SQLAlchemy failure). Code, status, message and `Retry-After: 5` are unchanged; the `database_error` log event carries the same `reason`. The MCP envelope and a REST request whose OAuth Bearer lookup itself hits the exhausted pool (#1981) do not carry it.

### Notes
- No migration. New environment variables, all optional with unchanged defaults: `DB_POOL_SIZE`, `DB_MAX_OVERFLOW`, `DB_POOL_TIMEOUT`, `SCHEDULER_LEADER_RETRY_SECONDS` (application) and `UVICORN_WORKERS`, `POSTGRES_MAX_CONNECTIONS` (single-server compose). Operators: raise `POSTGRES_MAX_CONNECTIONS` (a Postgres restart) before `UVICORN_WORKERS`; on a split host the Postgres `command:` line is carried to the data host by hand. With `UVICORN_WORKERS > 1`, legacy-era MCP sessions (the `initialize` handshake) and the MCP judgement caches are per worker; the stateless protocol revision is unaffected.

## [v0.103.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.103.0) — 2026-10-14

Context lifecycle tools on the default MCP URL, and a per-workspace add-on for the daily memory-creation limit.

### Added
- **`extra_memories_per_day` add-on** ([#1975](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1975)): the per-day memory creation limit (#1549) was tier-fixed. Admins can now grant extra daily capacity to one workspace (+100 memories/day per unit) from **Admin → Plans → Workspaces**, through `PUT /api/v1/admin/plans/workspaces/{id}/quotas` (`addon_memories_per_day_bonus`) or the internal billing push (`memories_per_day`). The quota panel shows the base, add-on and effective daily limit.

### Changed
- **Core MCP tool profile lists `create_context`, `update_context` and `delete_context`** ([#1973](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1973)): a client on the default `/mcp` URL can create, rename and delete contexts without reconnecting with `?profile=full`. The core profile is now 21 tools (≈40k characters); `merge_contexts` and `update_search_config` stay full-only. The empty-account hint from `list_contexts` now points at a `?tools=` allowlist as the reason `create_context` might be missing.

### Notes
- Migration `e97_1975_memories_per_day_addon` adds `workspaces.addon_memories_per_day_bonus` (default 0) and extends the `workspace_addons.addon_type` CHECK. No new environment variables, no operator action.
- Clients that cache `tools/list` (ChatGPT until Refresh, Codex per `initialize`) see the three context tools at their next read.

## [v0.102.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.102.0) — 2026-10-14

The last piece of the cancel policy: a Free workspace that stays over the Free capacity for 30 days has its data deleted by a daily sweep, with countdown mails before and a completion mail after. Only the clock starts on upgrade; the mails and the deletion ship switched off.

### Added
- **30-day purge of workspaces over the Free capacity** ([#1969](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1969)): a daily sweep (04:00 UTC, after the file GC) stamps `workspaces.capacity_locked_since` while the capacity-over lock is on and clears it when it is off, so a cleanup or a re-subscription restarts the clock. On the 30th calendar day it soft-deletes every memory, context and file of the workspace (the default context is kept, emptied) in 1000-row chunks, at most five workspaces per run, oldest first; the account stays and Free remains usable. The owner gets "N days left" mails on days 0/7/14/23/27/29 (ja/en) and a completion mail; the lock payload of `GET /api/v1/workspace/usage/current` gains `purge_at`, and the lock banner shows the date once purging is enabled. Safety rules: no deletion without a real mail provider, a first notice at least 7 days earlier and the final notice on an earlier day; the lock is re-checked before every context and chunk, and a re-subscription, an erasure or the owner's own cleanup mid-run aborts the purge; a `capacity_purge_started` audit row is committed before any deletion and a `capacity_purge` row after it.

### Notes
- Migration `e96_1969_ws_cap_locked_since` adds three nullable columns and a partial index to `workspaces`; no backfill. New environment variables `CAPACITY_PURGE_NOTICE_ENABLED` and `CAPACITY_PURGE_ENABLED`, both default `false`. Operators: stamping runs on upgrade; enable the mails, then the deletion, one deploy apart, following the rollout section of `docs/deployment.md`. Follow-ups filed as backlog #1971.

## [v0.101.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.101.0) — 2026-10-13

The storage side of the plan-change work: deleting a context frees its files, the capacity-over lock and the downgrade-eligibility check read one storage figure, and two gaps in the plan-seat and memory-row invariants are closed.

### Added
- **Deleting a context frees its files, and a context's files can be cleared in bulk** ([#1960](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1960)): `delete_context` soft-deletes the context's files with it and releases their storage at once, so an owner under the capacity-over lock no longer has to delete files one by one; `merge_contexts(delete_source=true)` moves the source's files to the target. The admin context restore brings the files back within 6 days (one day short of the file GC) unless the same content was uploaded again, and reports what it did. New owner-only `DELETE /api/v1/contexts/{id}/files` and a storage-page action clear a context's files without deleting it — the way to clear the undeletable default context. The file GC keeps an R2 object that a live re-upload of the same content still points at.

### Changed
- **One storage figure for the lock and eligibility** ([#1961](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1961)): the capacity-over lock and the downgrade-eligibility read both count the files a workspace can still shed (uploaded, not deleted, in a live context or workspace-scoped), so "eligible after cleanup" and "locked until cleanup" can no longer disagree; the upload quota keeps its own counter.

### Fixed
- **A seat-less creator is refused on their own private context** ([#1957](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1957)): the plan-seat gate of v0.100.0 applies to a private context's creator too; the workspace owner (by `owner_user_id`, even without a membership row) still passes, and the plan page says the owner can delete such contexts.
- **`memories.workspace_id` is NOT NULL** ([#1951](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1951)): migration `e95_1951_memories_ws_not_null` backfills the column from the memory's context (and re-queues those rows for embedding), purges soft-deleted rows that have neither workspace nor context, and refuses to run while a live memory has neither — see the pre-check in `docs/deployment.md`.

### Notes
- Migration `e95_1951_memories_ws_not_null` runs on upgrade (backfill + `SET NOT NULL`, `lock_timeout` 10s); run the pre-check SQL from `docs/deployment.md` first on a large `memories` table. No new environment variables. Operators: the context restore window for files is 6 days; the GC retention stays 7.
- Follow-ups filed for v1.0.0: #1963 (compound FK for `memories (workspace_id, context_id)`), #1966 (backfill files left in contexts deleted before #1960, then move the upload counter onto the shared figure).

## [v0.100.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.100.0) — 2026-10-13

The plan-change work of v0.99.0 completed: a workspace that drops below what it holds keeps everything, and the plan's seats and writable context slots are decided at use time; the capacity-over lock moved into the context resolvers; two gaps in the paused-feature set are closed.

### Added
- **Seats and writable slots when a plan drops below what a workspace holds** ([#1943](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1943)): members are ranked owner first, then earliest joined; the first `max_members` keep a seat and a member without one is refused on every workspace route (`403 FEAT-001`, `feature: members`, with the tier that would seat everyone). Live contexts are ranked oldest first; the first `max_contexts` stay writable and a context without a slot refuses content writes only (`FEAT-001`, `feature: contexts`) — remember, update, patch, uploads, agent state, edges, measurements, feedback and search settings — while reads, listing, export, delete, metadata updates and merge keep working so the owner can get back under the cap. Nothing is stored or deleted: re-subscribing, adding seats, or removing members / contexts restores everything on the next call, and paid → paid overage follows the same ranking. `GET /workspaces/{id}/plan` lists `members` / `contexts` under `suspended`, the members list carries `seat_paused` (and is ordered by the seat ranking), the contexts list `writes_paused`, and the web banner names both.

### Changed
- **The capacity-over lock is decided in the context resolvers** ([#1948](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1948)): `PermissionService.resolve_context_for_workspace_read` / `check_context_access` take a required `capacity` policy (`BLOCKED` runs the #1941 lock after every authorization step, `ALLOWED` never reads it), so every context-addressed REST route is gated in one place and a new route must choose; the MCP dispatcher table keeps deciding for MCP tools. A route table test pins which REST routes are gated, which are cleanup, and which rely on the service layer. Resource-token ingest is also refused while the owning workspace is locked.

### Fixed
- **One definition of a workspace's live memory count** ([#1946](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1946)): the memory quota gate, the capacity lock, the downgrade check, the admin plan list and MCP `get_usage` count the same thing (live rows of that workspace); the quota gate counted across workspaces before.
- **A connector's API key is refused while its connectors are paused** ([#1944](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1944)): a `connector:*` key answers `401` on every call once the plan no longer includes connectors (the worker dispatch was already cut off); the reserved `connector:` name prefix is refused on user-created keys.

### Notes
- No migration, no new environment variables.
- Self-hosted operators: before raising a workspace's plan, check `PLAN_FREE_MAX_MEMBERS_PER_WORKSPACE` / `PLAN_FREE_MAX_CONTEXTS_PER_WORKSPACE` — an admin-managed Free workspace with several members now seats only the owner (`docs/deployment.md`).
- Follow-ups filed for v1.0.0: #1951, #1952, #1954, #1956, #1957.

## [v0.99.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.99.0) — 2026-10-10

Plans and billing state now shape what a workspace can do: a running subscription blocks deletion, paid-only features pause (never delete) when a plan drops them, and a workspace that comes back to Free over its capacity is locked until it is cleaned up or re-subscribed.

### Added
- **Capacity-over lock** ([#1941](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1941)): a workspace back on Free from a subscription (`entitlement_source=external_billing`) that holds more live memories or storage than the Free limits pauses search and saving — recall, reference, explore, pinned, remember, update, uploads, context creation, graph, analyses, agent state, feedback, share-key recall and public search answer `403 CAPACITY-001` (`details.gate: "capacity"`, how much is over and a cleanup URL; non-members and public readers get a redacted form). Listing, deleting (incl. MCP `forget`), export and guardrails keep working, and the lock lifts as soon as usage fits. Self-hosted and admin-managed Free workspaces are never locked. The web UI shows a banner with the numbers, and `GET /api/v1/workspace/usage/current` and MCP `get_usage` carry `capacity_lock`.
- **Bulk delete** ([#1941](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1941)): `POST /api/v1/memory/forget-bulk` deletes the memories of one context that match date / type / tags filters (or `all: true`), with a dry-run count, at most 2,000 rows per request and a resume `cursor`; same permissions as `/memory/forget`. The context page gets a "Delete by filter" dialog and an Export button.
- **Pre-expiry capacity notice** ([#1941](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1941)): `POST /internal/workspaces/{id}/capacity-lock-notice` (billing service token) emails the owner when the workspace would be locked once its plan returns to Free; at most once per period and once per 24 hours.

### Changed
- **Deletion is blocked while a paid subscription runs** ([#1940](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1940)): deleting a workspace, or requesting / confirming account erasure while owning one, answers `409 BILLING-005` until the subscription has ended (a scheduled cancellation counts as running). The erasure sweep re-checks and fails a self-service request instead of erasing; admin force-erase and admin user delete stay allowed and log the override. The billing plan push refuses a paid plan onto a soft-deleted workspace (404).
- **Paid-only features pause when a plan drops them** ([#1939](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1939)): when a plan's allowance for a feature is 0 (typically back on Free), connector and resource-token ingest (`FEAT-001`), worker dispatch, indexing, Sleep's LLM passes and anonymous public search pause; nothing is deleted and a plan that includes them resumes everything. Retention phases keep running, held-back indexing never drops events, and the owner sees which features are paused (`GET /workspaces/{id}/plan` → `suspended`).
- **Resource indexing honours the workspace memory limit** ([#1939](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1939)): a batch applies as far as the limit allows and the rest waits.

### Fixed
- **Direct member add enforces the plan** ([#1939](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1939)): `POST /workspaces/{id}/members` now checks the `team_invitations` feature and the seat cap, like invitations.
- **A zero memory limit refuses the first memory** ([#1939](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1939)).
- **`forget` by query** ([#1941](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1941)) no longer fails on a missing workspace argument, and deletes with the same isolation as `forget` by id.

### Notes
- No migration, no new environment variables, no operator action.
- The billing service can now call the capacity-notice endpoint; its contract is in the #1941 issue comments.
- On `M`, Sleep and anonymous public serving are paused too (both allowances are 0 there), unless a self-hosted `PLAN_<KEY>_FEATURES` override includes `public_contexts`.

## [v0.98.1](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.98.1) — 2026-10-08

Hotfix for the v0.98.0 save skills.

### Fixed
- **Save skills always send `content`** ([#1936](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1936)): v0.98.0 let `/kagura-memory:session-summary` (`light`, step 4), `/kagura-memory:remember` and the Codex skill leave `content` out when the summary held the whole fact. `remember` and every `remember_batch` item require it, so such a save failed with `missing_fields`. `content` is now always present: when the summary holds the fact, it is one short line naming the evidence or source.

### Notes
- No migration, no new environment variables, no operator action.

## [v0.98.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.98.0) — 2026-10-08

Faster, lighter memory saving: the session summary writes as much as the session deserves, earlier sessions can be turned into memories afterwards, and forgetting a memory no longer reads as an edit in the change log.

### Added
- **`/kagura-memory:digest`** ([#1931](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1931)): turns this project's earlier Claude Code sessions — the ones that ended without a session summary — into memories the user picks. The user runs it, best in a fresh session. A bundled standard-library script lists the sessions, leaving out the running one and any written to in the last 10 minutes (an open session idle longer is listed; the user picks which to digest). It reduces each transcript to the user's and the assistant's words (no tool calls, tool output or thinking), redacts secret shapes and caps the length. The skill never reads a raw transcript. Kept memories carry `source_uri="claude-session://<session_id>"`. Transcripts stay on the machine, and nothing schedules or launches the command.
- **Session-summary save modes** ([#1930](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1930)): `/kagura-memory:session-summary [light|standard|deep|auto]`, default `auto`. The mode sets three things: how many candidates are proposed (3 / 7 / about 15), which fields each one gets, and whether the pin, tool-guardrail and keep-current steps run. `auto` picks the mode by written criteria and announces its pick in one line.

### Changed
- **Save skills write each thing once** ([#1930](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1930)): `/kagura-memory:remember`, `/kagura-memory:session-summary` and the Codex skill change how each memory is written:
  - `content` holds only what the summary leaves out.
  - `context_summary` is one sentence on when to recall it.
  - Tags are capped at about 6, with no spelling variants.
  - Related issues are `issue:#N` tags instead of a `Related issues:` line in `content`.
  The wait at the end of a session is the model writing these fields, so it shortens.

### Fixed
- **`forget` no longer reads as an edit in `changes_since`** ([#1924](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1924)): a soft delete no longer stamps `updated_at`, so the log lists `created` and `forgotten` only. Tombstones written before this release are also filtered: the `updated` lane keeps only edits made before `deleted_at`.

### Notes
- No migration, no new environment variables, no operator action.
- A context whose usage guide asks for a `Related issues:` line in `content` can drop that request: the skills now tag issues instead.

## [v0.97.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.97.0) — 2026-10-06

Follow-ups to the v0.96.0 sign-in and resource-token work: a password sign-in no longer waits behind a dead lock longer than the lock lives, the resource-token cap and quota ceiling count the same per-workspace population and cannot be overshot by concurrent mints, and the MCP smoke-test runbook was run live against v0.96.1.

### Changed
- **The resource-token cap counts per workspace** ([#1919](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1919)): the active-token cap (`max_resource_tokens`) and the quota ceiling (`max_resource_tokens` × 10,000 events/hour) now count the same set — the workspace's active, non-connector tokens, attributed through their resource — in REST create, MCP `setup_resource`, the token list and the downgrade-eligibility read. Before, the cap counted per creator, so two owners could each mint a full set and then have every quota raise refused. `PATCH` / `DELETE /api/v1/resource-tokens/{id}` find the token with the same predicate, so every counted token is addressable; a token whose resource belongs to another workspace is a uniform 404 (was 403). The MCP token list keeps omitting legacy tokens with no resource (they cannot authenticate); the REST list shows them.
- **The smoke-test runbook matches a live run** ([#1920](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1920)): `/kagura-memory:smoke-test` passed 77/77 main rows, 10/10 Agent Control Plane rows and 7/7 resource rows against v0.96.1 with the full tool profile; two expected shapes were corrected (a refused atomic `remember_batch` reports `failed=1, skipped=1`; every event of a forgotten memory omits `summary`).

### Fixed
- **A dead sign-in lock delays a password sign-in by at most its lifetime** ([#1918](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1918)): the per-account session-sweep lock lives 5 s (was 10 s) and a waiting sign-in waits it out (was: gave up after 2 s and answered 503 while the lock still lived). A per-account fence makes a sweep that a later sign-in overtook delete nothing, so it can no longer remove the later sign-in's session. A Redis connection failure during the lock's release or the fenced sweep is reported as a Redis failure, not as a lost lease.
- **Concurrent mints cannot exceed the resource-token cap** ([#1927](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1927)): REST create and MCP `setup_resource` take a per-workspace transaction lock before counting, so two owners minting at the same moment can no longer each pass the count.

### Notes
- No migration, no new environment variables, no operator action.
- Behaviour changes: a workspace whose owners together hold `max_resource_tokens` active tokens can mint no more, whoever minted them; workspaces already over the cap from per-creator minting keep their tokens (nothing is revoked) and must revoke or lower before raising a quota. A sign-in whose session sweep is overtaken by a later sign-in of the same account answers 503 (try again).

## [v0.96.1](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.96.1) — 2026-10-06

Hardening of sign-in, account linking, context access and log hygiene (PR #1921). Every new check fails closed. No database migration and no new environment variables; operators should read the new `docs/deployment.md` sections listed under Notes.

### Fixed
- **A removed sign-in provider stays removed**: a Google or GitHub identity whose link was removed from an account can no longer sign in to it; the login page explains that the method was removed. `docs/deployment.md` has a review query for accounts whose original provider was unlinked.
- **Provider linking completes only in the browser session that started it**: `link-provider` needs a session, and a callback arriving from another session links nothing and redirects to `/profile?error=link_failed`.
- **Second-factor attempts count against the sign-in budget**: wrong TOTP codes share the per-account budget with wrong passwords (one atomic reservation per attempt, 429 when spent), a correct password no longer resets it, an accepted code cannot be replayed, and the owner is notified when sign-in is paused.
- **The primary provider follows the account's original identity**: an OAuth account adopts a primary provider only from the identity it was created with; linking or unlinking never makes a later-attached provider primary, and `refresh-oauth` says which sign-in restores it.
- **REST `recall` resolves the context for the caller** like every other read path (422 for a malformed `filters.context_id`, uniform 404 when the caller cannot open the context), linked-account widening in search and tag resolution applies only to contexts the caller could open as a member, and leaving a workspace clears it as the member's current workspace.
- **Credentials stay out of the logs**: the single-server Caddy template drops the `X-Resource-Api-Key` header from both log filters, and workspace invitation tokens are redacted in `/invitations/<token>`, `/invite/<token>` and percent-encoded `return_to` shapes in the proxy filters, the application scrubber and `usage_stats`.

### Notes
- No database migration, no new environment variables.
- **Operator action** (self-hosted): regenerate the Caddyfile and restart Caddy to apply the log filters; then follow the new `docs/deployment.md` sections "Credential headers in the proxy log", "Workspace invitation links", "Removed sign-in providers" and "Which sign-in provider syncs the account's email" (re-issue resource tokens that went through the proxy, purge old container logs, review the listed accounts).
- Behaviour changes: an identity whose link was removed must be linked again from the profile page before it can sign in; a provider attached after account creation never syncs the account email; cross-workspace REST `recall` follows the same path as MCP instead of returning an empty result.

## [v0.96.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.96.0) — 2026-10-05

Eighteen follow-ups from the review of v0.82.1–v0.94.0: sign-in and account linking hold up under contention and against a freshly attached provider, resource tokens and memory health respect the workspace boundary, the embedding migration and `remember_batch` stop mis-counting and mis-building, and the MCP docs and smoke-test runbook match the 70-tool registry.

### Changed
- **Reconnecting Slack in the same workspace refreshes the bot token** ([#1880](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1880)): installing a Slack team that this workspace already connected stores the install's bot token on the existing connector and drops its cached channel pages; the 409 carries `details.token_refreshed: true` only when the token was saved, and the UI says "refreshed" only then (a manual bind says the pasted token was not saved). The public-only channel marker no longer applies to a first page, a missing-scope notice lists what each missing scope disables, and a connector without a display name is never shown as its UUID.
- **MCP tool counts and guide text follow the registry** ([#1883](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1883)): the docs state 70 tools, 18 in the core profile and 36 read-only, pinned by a test that reads the registry. `bootstrap` documents `include` and `max_chars` and states its `since` range ('0d' to '999d'), `describe_tools` ignores blank names, and the maintain skill says nothing changes without a pick and reports `dismissed` counts.
- **The smoke-test runbook covers the v0.93.0 surface** ([#1886](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1886)): it requires the full tool profile and a workspace owner, starts with a read-only `describe_tools` check, exercises `remember_batch`, `list`, `changes_since` and `bootstrap`, and its coverage table is checked against the registry by a test.
- **CI runs the tests it was skipping** ([#1885](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1885)): the integration job fails instead of skipping when its database is unavailable (`REQUIRE_TEST_DATABASE=1`), every one-shot CLI is covered by the logging test, and the OAuth off-loop check reads the code's structure. Device confirmation and the consent lookup run on the bounded OAuth pool.

### Fixed
- **`remember_batch` quota, timeout and reply accounting** ([#1873](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1873)): an atomic batch reserves its daily quota once, all or nothing, and gives it back on rollback; a batch cancelled after its commit answers `committed_after_timeout: true` with every `memory_id` instead of a timeout; `skipped` is counted apart from `failed`; an undeclared item key is a per-item `invalid_argument`; and the duplicate check uses the same sharing scope as search, marking another member's candidate `supersedable: false`.
- **Memory health counts `remember_batch` and respects membership** ([#1874](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1874)): batch writes count as writes (new `remember_batch_calls` metric), and a linked account's private context appears only when the caller could open it as themselves.
- **Identity-link follow-ups** ([#1875](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1875)): a provider attached within the sign-in window cannot prove its account (`link_proof=recent_provider`) and does not become the account's primary provider; merging two link sets audits and notifies every affected account; a linked sign-in shows the owner's name; and an OAuth account without a primary provider gets it back on a sign-in through an established link.
- **`changes_since` omits a forgotten memory's summary** ([#1876](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1876)): events of a forgotten memory carry `memory_id`, `kind` and `at` only (plus `superseded_by`), in `bootstrap` too. The `updated` kind is documented as including Sleep maintenance, and a `details.<key>` filter on `list` matches the stored JSON type as documented.
- **Resource tokens stay inside their workspace** ([#1877](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1877)): the auto-revoke on context delete and re-slug is scoped to the workspace's own resource, the quota ceiling on update sums the workspace's tokens, the MCP token list shows tokens of a resource whose contexts were deleted, and a schema cannot be published for a resource with no live context (409).
- **Sign-in and device approval no longer wait on a row lock without bound** ([#1878](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1878)): the known-device record waits at most 5 s and never fails the sign-in, overlapping password sign-ins leave exactly one live session (the other answers 503, try again), a wrong TOTP code no longer clears unrelated state, and the device page handles 401 and 503.
- **Security notices leave out an admin's details** ([#1879](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1879)): a notice about an admin's action on a member no longer shows the admin's IP address or device, a bad buffered entry cannot stop the flush job, and the reset page says connected apps and MCP clients are disconnected too.
- **A Redis password that breaks `REDIS_URL` is caught before a restart** ([#1881](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1881)): the documented pre-restart check parses the rendered URL and compares its password with Redis's, and the API reports an unparseable URL with a fixed message that never contains the password.
- **Former members' emails and an integer user id** ([#1882](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1882)): workspace sleep reports resolve emails for current members only, and admin user stats return the public `user_id` in a typed `user` object.
- **Frontend small fixes** ([#1884](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1884)): the profile names every sign-in method ("Google, Email and password"), admin plan badges expose the role to screen readers, the filtered-empty context list has its own message, and earlier connector apps keep a translated label.
- **Graph stats count a linked pair once** ([#1895](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1895)): graph stats, top connections and the memory-health edge counts no longer double count a pair that two linked accounts both hold.
- **The embedding migration handles resource-ingested memories** ([#1896](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1896)): re-embed rebuilds a document's vector under its own point id instead of a label vector under the row id, verify and switch no longer remove it as stale, and rows that cannot be rebuilt are skipped and listed on an `UNREBUILDABLE` line without failing the migration.
- **An unrebuildable resource row fails once** ([#1897](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1897)): it ends `failed` on the first attempt (`embedding_resource_unrebuildable`) instead of spending the retry budget, a context restore warns about such rows, and the admin retry rebuilds them once the schema is published again.
- **Session store logs name only the Redis host and port** ([#1898](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1898)): the session manager and the Redis pool no longer log the URL, so a password in the userinfo or the query never reaches the logs.

### Migration
- **Admin user stats** (`GET /api/v1/admin/users/{user_id}/stats`): `user.id` (integer) is gone; read `user.user_id` (string).
- **`remember_batch`**: `failed` no longer includes skipped items (read `skipped`), an over-quota atomic batch is a top-level `quota_exceeded`, and `dedupe_unavailable` is recorded as 503.
- **`changes_since` / `bootstrap`**: clients must accept an event without `summary`.
- **`list` filters**: `details.<key>` no longer matches across JSON types (`2` does not match `"2"`).

### Notes
- No database migration and no new environment variables.
- **Operator action**: none required. If the Redis password contains `/ # ? [ ]` or `%`, run the render check in `docs/deployment.md` before the next restart.
- Unrebuildable resource rows now count in the existing "stalled" embedding figures.

## [v0.95.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.95.0) — 2026-10-05

Seven correctness fixes: the operator password reset now contains an incident the way the emailed reset does, linked accounts can explore a shared private context again, and the resource pipeline no longer loses or mis-builds vectors on a backlog or a restore.

### Fixed
- **The CLI password reset signs the account out everywhere** ([#1866](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1866)): `reset_password` (choices 1 and 3) now revokes OAuth / MCP tokens and pending authorization and device codes, forgets known devices and signs out every browser session before the hash write commits, and prints the counts. It refuses to start when the session store (Redis, `REDIS_URL`) cannot be reached, and rolls the database back and exits non-zero when the session delete or the commit fails — the session store is not part of the database transaction, so after a failed commit the password is unchanged while the sessions are already signed out, and the reset has to be run again; messages name only the store's host and port and the error type. "Disable MFA only" revokes nothing, by decision. API keys and OAuth client secrets are not revoked.
- **`explore` with two linked accounts on one memory pair** ([#1867](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1867)): when both accounts of an identity-link set held an edge for the same pair in a private context, `explore` failed instead of returning the related memories. A link-set read now takes the strongest row per pair, and the edge lists, the graph data route, `list_edges` and node degree count the pair once.
- **Sleep and the embedding status handle never-edited memories** ([#1868](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1868)): importance re-evaluation skipped every memory whose `updated_at` is NULL, and the dedup candidate fetch and the failed-embedding list ordered them arbitrarily above the cap. All three fall back to `created_at` (the failure list to the embedding clock) with a stable order.
- **The orphan vector sweep spares an in-flight resource batch** ([#1869](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1869)): resource points carry `indexed_at`, the real write time, and the sweep's grace period is measured from the later of `indexed_at` and `updated_at`, so a backlog batch whose rows have not committed is no longer swept. `sweep_orphan_vectors --apply` reports what a refused delete pass had already deleted, and the shared-point check on forget uses an indexed `EXISTS`.
- **Restoring a resource context rebuilds the documents' vectors** ([#1870](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1870)): a resource-ingested memory set back to pending (context restore, re-embed, admin re-queue) is rebuilt as the indexer builds it — document text, the per-document point id and the resource payload — instead of a label vector under the row id. A row that cannot be rebuilt (resource schema gone, content no longer a JSON document) ends `failed` with the reason. The restore counts use the same workspace scope as the update.
- **`tags_normalize` keeps number tags distinct on write** ([#1871](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1871)): `remember` / `remember_batch` with `tags_normalize=true` stored `v0.11.0` as an existing `v0.1.10` (and `pr-123` as `pr-1-23`) because both fold to one form. A tag is now mapped only onto a stored spelling with the same numbers; otherwise it is stored as written. The read-side filter still widens by the fold alone.
- **`transfer_context_creator` moves declared and supersedes edges** ([#1872](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1872)): the old account's declared, `supersedes` / `contradicts` and sleep-discovered edges move with the context (Hebbian weights stay), in the transfer and in the `--repair-payloads` sweep, so the new owner can list, update and delete them. The sweep no longer needs the `--from` user row, a memory that is not embedded yet is skipped instead of counted as a payload failure, and the plan and result lines name what changed.

### Notes
- **Migration**: `e94_1869_summary_emb_idx` adds a partial index on `memories.summary_embedding_id`, built concurrently: reads and writes on `memories` are not blocked (it still takes the lock that keeps other schema changes on the table waiting).
- **Operator action**: the CLI password reset now needs the session store; run it where `REDIS_URL` points at the Redis the API uses. Resource points written before this release have no `indexed_at` and keep the previous sweep behaviour.
- **API notes**: the failed-memory rows of the embedding status endpoint gain a nullable `embedding_attempted_at`. No new environment variables.

## [v0.94.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.94.0) — 2026-10-05

Two admin tables now agree on how a row is labelled, and a resource token can always be found and revoked by a workspace owner — including after the resource's contexts were deleted.

### Added
- **Cost aggregation rows name the workspace and the user** ([#1861](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1861)): `GET /api/v1/admin/cost-aggregation` and `GET /api/v1/workspaces/{id}/cost-aggregation` add `workspace_name` and `user_email` to every row, batch-resolved per response the way the sleep-reports routes resolve their labels (two queries, no N+1; a deleted workspace or a connector identity leaves the field null). `/admin/cost` renders the workspace name and the user's email (fallback `uid:<8>`, the short id in the tooltip) and links the user to the admin user page; `/workspace/cost` shows the same labels as plain text and resolves emails for current workspace members only.

### Fixed
- **Resource tokens stay reachable after their context is deleted** ([#1863](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1863)): token list, update and revoke used to require a live context, so soft-deleting a resource's last context left its tokens active for ingest yet invisible and unrevocable (403). The routes now judge the workspace boundary by the token's own workspace and its `resources` row (`resource_pk`), and token management is workspace-scoped: an owner lists and revokes every token in the workspace, whoever minted it. `GET /api/v1/resources` keeps listing a resource whose contexts were all deleted while it holds active tokens (context fields null); the resources page marks such a row "Context deleted" and its detail page skips the indexer and points at the Tokens tab, where the tokens can be revoked. Once the last token is revoked the row disappears. Minting a token still requires a live context, and the 403 now says so.

### Notes
- **API notes**: cost-aggregation rows gain two nullable string fields. `ResourceListItem.context_id` / `context_name` are nullable (null only for a context-less resource with active tokens). `GET /api/v1/resource-tokens` returns the workspace's tokens rather than the caller's own, and its `resource_id` filter is pinned to the resource in the caller's workspace. `PATCH` / `DELETE /api/v1/resource-tokens/{id}` on a token of another workspace return 404 (previously 403).
- No migration, no new environment variables, no operator action.

## [v0.93.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.93.0) — 2026-10-05

The MCP surface gets cheaper to carry and quicker to start: the bare `/mcp` URL lists the core tools by default, every core description is a few lines with a `guide` tool for the manual, a session starts with one `bootstrap` call, writes acknowledge compactly, and `list` / `changes_since` / `remember_batch` take the round trips out of inventories, catch-up and end-of-session saves.

### Added
- **`guide` — the tool manual on demand** ([#1850](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1850)): `guide(["remember"])` (or any topic) returns the usage text the core descriptions used to carry; `guide()` lists the topics. Every core description is now three to five lines with its `Returns` keys and a `Manual:` pointer, and a caller error carries `help: 'Manual: guide(["<tool>"])'`.
- **`describe_tools` — the tools the URL left out** ([#1849](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1849)): names and one-line summaries of the tools not listed by the current profile (`query` narrows, `names` returns full schemas), so a session can discover Sleep, analyses, files, edges, secrets, resources and the agent control plane and reconnect with `?profile=full` when it needs them.
- **`list` and `changes_since`** ([#1852](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1852)): the deterministic siblings of `recall`. `list(context_id, filters, order_by, direction, cursor, limit, include_details)` returns every live memory matching exact filters (recall's vocabulary plus `details.<key>` equality) in a stable order with `total`; `changes_since(context_id, since, until, kinds, cursor, limit)` returns the memory-level change log — `created`, `updated`, `superseded` (with `superseded_by`), `forgotten` — oldest first with a keyset cursor. No ranking, no embedding, no Hebbian write.
- **`bootstrap` — a session start in one call** ([#1851](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1851)): `bootstrap(context_id, since?, include?, max_chars?)` returns the context block, its guardrails, the pinned set, upcoming Time Memories and the changes since a time (default `"7d"`), each bounded and fail-soft (`degraded`). The `kagura-memory` session-start skill and the Codex skill call it when the tool list has it and fall back to the seven-call sequence on an older server.
- **`remember_batch`, `tags_normalize` and `dedupe`** ([#1853](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1853)): `remember_batch(context_id, items[1..50], atomic?)` stores several memories in one call with per-item results (all or nothing with `atomic=true`); `remember(tags_normalize=true)` stores each tag under the context's established spelling and reports the mapping in `lint` as `tag_normalized`; `remember(dedupe="check")` returns the nearest live memory above the suggestion threshold as `duplicate_candidate` without writing, `dedupe="off"` writes and never suggests. The session-summary skills use `remember_batch` when it is listed.

### Changed
- **The bare `/mcp` URL lists the core tools** ([#1849](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1849)): `tools/list` returns the 18 core tools (≈ 35k characters) by default; `?profile=full` lists all 70 (≈ 102k). The Web UI's connection snippets gain an *All tools* switch that writes `?profile=full`. The empty-contexts hint, the skills and the docs say how to reconnect for the rest.
- **Compact write acknowledgements** ([#1851](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1851)): `remember` / `update_memory` return `persistence` as `{scope, committed, promotes_via, consolidation_archive_min_age_days}`; the lifecycle prose comes back only with `verbose=true` (`persistence.detail`) and lives in `guide(["persistence"])`. `recall` omits an empty `related_tags` and an empty per-result `tags`.
- **Tool descriptions trimmed to the contract** ([#1850](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1850)): the core list shrank from ≈ 33k to ≈ 25k characters before the new tools were added; the security rule, the supersede guidance and the recall signals an agent acts on stay in the descriptions and are pinned by tests.

### Fixed
- **Plan-change dialogs use the configured plan names** ([#1848](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1848)): the admin user and plan pages render plan labels through `PlanBadge` / `planLabelFromEnv` instead of hard-coded tier strings.
- **Every freshly embedded memory read as edited** ([#1852](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1852)): the embedding claim and failure stamps wrote `updated_at`, so `updated_at > created_at` held for virtually every row. The pipeline now keeps its own clock, `memories.embedding_attempted_at`; `updated_at` is written by edits and promotions only. A `supersedes` upsert over an existing edge of another type re-dates the edge, so `changes_since` reports the supersession when it was declared.

### Notes
- **Migration** `e93_1852_embedding_attempted_at`: adds the nullable `memories.embedding_attempted_at`; no backfill (a NULL clock reads as stale / retry-eligible, as before).
- **Migration notes for MCP clients**: a connection on the bare `/mcp` URL now lists the core tools — add `?profile=full` to the stored URL for a client that should see every schema (the MCP registry entry keeps the bare URL, so registry installs get the core profile); connectors re-read the list on Refresh / initialize; the guardrail digest suffix names `get_context_info`. `persistence.detail` is absent unless `verbose=true`. Read `recall`'s `related_tags` and a result's `tags` with `.get`. `tags_normalize` defaults to `false` in this release and is planned to flip in the next.
- No new environment variables; no operator action beyond the migration.

## [v0.92.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.92.0) — 2026-10-03

Linked accounts see the same graph and health picture inside a private context, a failed Confirm with Google says why, forget removes a resource-ingested memory's vector point, the OAuth routes stop blocking the event loop, and the credentials page is rebuilt around one MCP connection card.

### Added
- **Graph, explore and memory health follow the identity-link set** ([#1834](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1834)): in a private context, `GET /graph/stats`, `GET /graph/data`, the `list_edges` tool and `explore` include a linked account's edges, so a seed written by the linked account no longer returns `seed_not_in_graph`. The memory-health report covers the caller's contexts plus the link set's private contexts, with every signal scoped the same way. Edge writes, deletes and Sleep stay per account.
- **Confirm with Google reports why a proof failed** ([#1833](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1833)): the Google callback returns to the Linked accounts card with `link_proof=unproved` (Google sent no authentication time) or `link_proof=stale` (older than the 10-minute window), and the card shows a distinct message for each. `GET /me/account/identity-links` gains `providers` (attached OAuth providers plus `"password"`) for the caller and each linkable account, and the *Confirm with Google* button is offered only when an account that still needs a proof can sign in with Google.

### Changed
- **Credentials page: one MCP connection card** ([#1836](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1836)): the page opens with the bare `/mcp` URL, the Claude Code OAuth one-liner and, for users in more than one workspace, a collapsed `/mcp/w/<id>` pin form. The *API keys* tab keeps the key-bearing snippets next to the key; the *Custom OAuth apps* tab drops the Claude and ChatGPT presets (connectors register through Dynamic Client Registration), and apps created from them are flagged as no longer needed. Docs and `.mcp.json.example` use the bare `/mcp` endpoint.
- **OAuth routes run their sync database work off the event loop** ([#1831](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1831)): client CRUD, dynamic client registration, authorize, device authorization and verify, introspect and revoke run the Authlib sync session in a bounded `oauth-sync` thread pool (10 workers) that copies the request context, so a row lock held by an async transaction no longer stalls the loop. Hiding or regenerating another owner's client returns 403 instead of 500, and `GET /oauth/clients/{id}` honours the visibility window. A test parses the module and fails if a sync session is opened inside an `async def` again.
- **"Profile Settings" is now "Settings"** ([#1837](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1837)): the user-menu item, the `/profile` page title and the legal copy that points at the page read *Settings* / 「設定」; keys and the route are unchanged.
- **The identity-link dialog says where a link applies** ([#1838](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1838)): the confirmation dialog and the Linked accounts card explain that linked accounts see each other's private contexts only inside workspaces both are members of.

### Fixed
- **`forget` removes a resource-ingested memory's vector point** ([#1829](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1829)): every vector delete (forget by id and by query, working-memory cleanup, the legacy archive task, dedup merge) uses `Memory.point_id`, the `uuid5(resource:doc:version)` id a resource-ingested row carries, instead of the row id. A point still owned by another live row is kept. The resource indexer writes the row's id into the point payload, and the orphan sweep now classifies resource points in live contexts (`resource_tombstoned`, `resource_no_row`, shown as "doc gone" in the CLI) instead of keeping them all. Leftover points are cleaned by the next daily sweep; run `cli/sweep_orphan_vectors.py` in dry-run first to see the count.
- **Flaky a11y lanes** ([#1824](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1824)): the dashboard spec waits for the KPI cards before scanning, the loading and empty-state texts get a dark-mode colour that passes contrast, and the shared contrast helper rescans after a `next dev` reload instead of failing with "Execution context was destroyed". The dashboard and admin loading blocks use the `SpinnerLoading` primitive.

### Notes
- No migration, no new environment variables, no operator action. Existing leftover resource vector points are removed by the next daily orphan sweep ([#1829](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1829)).

## [v0.91.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.91.0) — 2026-10-02

Identity links and sessions get stricter and easier to manage: a link needs a proved credential rather than any recent sign-in, an account can leave a link set, a password reset can no longer race a sign-in, and the memory-health report stops flagging contexts that are simply unused.

### Added
- **Leave an identity-link set** ([#1807](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1807)): `POST /api/v1/me/account/identity-links/leave` and a *Leave* button on the profile page remove only the caller's account from a set of three or more; the other accounts stay linked. Unlink and leave audit and notify every account the removal separates.
- **Confirm with Google before a link** ([#1818](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1818)): the *Linked accounts* card can start a Google sign-in with `link_proof=1`, which asks Google for `auth_time` and records it from the verified ID token as that account's proof.

### Changed
- **An identity link needs a proved credential** ([#1818](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1818)): both accounts must have proved their credential within the 10-minute window — a password or MFA sign-in, or a Google `link_proof` sign-in whose ID token carries a recent `auth_time`. A plain OAuth sign-in, a GitHub sign-in, or a Google token without `auth_time` no longer counts. `IDENTITY_LINK_ALLOW_OAUTH_SIGNIN_PROOF=true` restores the v0.90.0 behaviour. Ordinary sign-ins send the same request as before.
- **Private surfaces follow the link set** ([#1807](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1807)): access patterns in a private context include the linked accounts' activity, and the private tag vocabulary is cached per link set, so a link change takes effect in every API process at once.
- **Sign-in providers and identity links are named apart** ([#1807](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1807)): identity-link endpoints move to the new `identity-links` OpenAPI tag; `account-linking` now describes only sign-in providers attached to one account.
- **Unused contexts are no longer flagged write-only** ([#1822](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1822)): the memory-health retrieval signal grades a context with no successful MCP reads or writes in the window OK with an `idle_store` note. `write_only_store` (WARN) now needs successful writes and no successful reads; failed calls are not counted and `update_memory` counts as a write. New metrics `successful_read_calls` and `successful_write_calls`.
- **Browser sessions are indexed per user** ([#1809](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1809)): password change, password reset and account erasure invalidate a user's sessions through a per-user Redis set instead of scanning every session key, and the active-session count no longer uses `KEYS`. Sessions created before the upgrade are still found by a catch-up scan during their remaining lifetime.

### Fixed
- **A password sign-in during a reset's session sweep no longer survives the reset** ([#1809](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1809)): the sign-in re-checks the password hash after writing its session and drops the session (401) if the hash changed; the MFA step does the same. A database error during the re-check returns 503.
- **Deleting an account keeps identity-link rows consistent** ([#1807](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1807)): `identity_links.linked_by` is a foreign key with `ON DELETE SET NULL`, so deleting the account that made a link no longer leaves a dangling id.
- **Alembic no longer disables the application's loggers** ([#1808](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1808)): `alembic/env.py` keeps existing loggers when it configures logging, which also fixes logging tests failing when migration tests share a pytest process.
- **`make coverage-upload` uses the backend virtual environment** ([#1809](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1809)) and checks its tools and token before running tests.
- **Flaky no-leak assertions** ([#1810](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1810)): transport-failure tests no longer fail when the random `correlation_id` happens to contain a port number.

### Notes
- **Migration** `e92_1807_linked_by_fk`: makes `identity_links.linked_by` nullable, sets values that name an account outside the row's own set to NULL, then adds the `ON DELETE SET NULL` foreign key.
- **New environment variable** `IDENTITY_LINK_ALLOW_OAUTH_SIGNIN_PROOF` (default `false`). Under the default, a Google account can be linked only when the OAuth app is published, Verified and has *Session age claims* turned on; GitHub accounts need the variable set to `true`. See `docs/deployment.md`, identity links.
- **Operator action**: run the session Redis with `maxmemory-policy noeviction` — an evicted per-user index would let a password reset miss live sessions. After a rollback and redeploy of this version, delete the `session_index:since` key so the catch-up scan runs again.
- The orphan vector sweep still keeps resource points in live contexts; the payload `memory_id` is not reliable for them ([#1808](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1808), follow-up [#1829](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1829)). Kagura Lite's LanceDB sweep paths are now tested against a real table.

## [v0.90.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.90.0) — 2026-10-02

Linked accounts and context deletion keep your data: a deleted context can be restored from its rows, linking two accounts needs a fresh sign-in of both, linked owners write to the same memories, and a linked provider no longer rewrites the account's email.

### Added
- **Restore a deleted context** ([#1804](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1804)): `POST /api/v1/admin/contexts/{context_id}/restore` (system admins; dry run by default, `new_name` to restore under another name) and `python -m src.cli.restore_context <id>` (plan by default, `--apply --yes`, `--name`, `--actor`) bring back a soft-deleted context and the memories its deletion removed, until the tombstone purge runs. Restored memories are re-embedded by the embedding sweep. Refused with 409 when the context is not deleted, its workspace is deleted, or a live context now uses its name or resource id. Each apply writes a `context_restore` audit row. Not restored: memories forgotten before the deletion, Sleep tombstones, purged rows, neural edges, members' `allowed_context_ids` entries and revoked resource tokens. See `docs/deployment.md`, *Restoring a deleted context*.
- **Identity links name themselves in the password-reset notice** ([#1803](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1803)): when the account has linked accounts, the email says a reset does not remove them and to review *Linked accounts* on the profile page.

### Changed
- **Linking two accounts needs a fresh sign-in of both** ([#1803](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1803)): `POST /api/v1/me/account/identity-links` requires the session user and the target to have signed in on this session within the last 10 minutes, and answers 403 (`AUTH-305`) otherwise. `GET` reports `signed_in_recently` per account and `sign_in_window_minutes`; Profile Settings shows when to sign in again. Sessions created before this release have no sign-in time, so the first link after upgrading asks for a new sign-in.
- **Linked owners write to the same memories in a private context** ([#1803](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1803)): an `external_id` upsert from either linked account replaces the same memory (every match, so duplicates left by earlier upserts are folded), and `linked_memory_ids`, `linked_source_uris` and `supersedes` may name the linked account's memories. Shared contexts are unchanged.
- **`delete_context` stamps the context and its memories with one deletion time** ([#1804](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1804)), so a restore takes exactly the memories the deletion removed. For a context deleted before this release it falls back to memories the same user deleted in the 10 minutes before, and the dry run says so with the count.

### Fixed
- **A linked provider's sign-in no longer rewrites the account's email** ([#1811](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1811)): only the account's primary provider (`auth_provider`) syncs email and name; a linked secondary provider updates nothing but its last-used time, sends no email-change notice, and no longer fails with 409 when its address belongs to another account. A secondary provider marks the email verified only when it attests the address already stored.
- **The orphan vector sweep no longer holds one long read transaction** ([#1804](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1804)): it ends the transaction after each page, so `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` cannot cut a long scan short.

### Notes
- `POST /api/v1/admin/contexts/recover` still rebuilds a context from surviving vector points; for a context whose rows still exist its error now points to the restore endpoint.
- An account whose `auth_provider` is empty (password-first accounts) no longer has its email or name synced from a linked OAuth provider.
- No migration, no new environment variables, no operator action.

## [v0.89.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.89.0) — 2026-10-02

Keys, tokens and invitations are addressed by opaque public ids instead of sequential row numbers, ahead of the 1.0 API freeze, and a sign-in through a linked provider opens the right account. This release also carries the v0.88.1 fix.

### Changed
- **Opaque public ids for keys, tokens and invitations** ([#1008](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1008)) — **breaking**: API keys (including member credential keys), share keys, resource tokens (including the connector `token_id`) and workspace invitations are identified by `akey_…`, `skey_…`, `rtok_…` and `winv_…` (22 base62 characters) in REST responses, path parameters and MCP tools, instead of their integer row ids. An integer path id is refused with 422. `ExternalKeyResponse.id` is removed; external keys stay addressed by `key_name`. New audit rows record the public id, and log lines carry `public_id` beside the internal id.
- **Integer row ids dropped from profile, OAuth client and admin responses** ([#1813](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1813)) — **breaking**: `GET /api/v1/users/profile` no longer returns `id` and now returns the string `user_id`; OAuth client responses (including the dynamic client registration response) no longer return `id` — use `client_id`; the system-admin list drops `id` and `initial_admin_id` — each entry's `is_initial_admin` marks the initial admin.

### Fixed
- **A sign-in through a linked provider opens the owning account** ([#1805](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1805)): both OAuth callbacks keyed the session by the provider's `sub`, so signing in with a provider linked to another account landed in a session that saw none of that account's workspaces and contexts. The session, the old-session invalidation, the personal-workspace and invitation steps and the profile-refresh check now use the account that owns the provider. Sessions opened through a linked provider before the fix are invalidated at the next sign-in. A database error while resolving the owner now fails the sign-in instead of opening a session for the `sub`.

### Migration
- **Database:** `alembic upgrade head` runs `e91_1008_public_ids`, which adds `public_id` to `api_keys`, `share_keys`, `resource_tokens` and `workspace_invitations` and backfills every row (revoked and expired ones included) under a 10-second lock timeout. The column has a database default, so an older instance still running during a rolling deploy can insert. Downgrading drops the ids for good; a later upgrade issues new ones.
- **API clients:** read ids as strings and address keys, tokens and invitations by them — before `DELETE /api/v1/resource-tokens/42`, now `DELETE /api/v1/resource-tokens/rtok_3fJ9…`. Use the Python SDK `kagura-memory` 0.42.1 or later, or the TypeScript SDK `kagura-memory` 0.14.1 or later; both accept the new ids and still work against earlier servers.
- **MCP:** `list_my_bindings`, `describe_binding`, `list_resource_tokens`, `setup_resource` and `setup_connector` return string ids; `describe_binding` takes the `key_id` string from `list_my_bindings`, and an integer gets `invalid_arguments`.
- **Profile, OAuth clients, system admins:** use `user_id`, `client_id` and `is_initial_admin` in place of the removed integer fields.
- **Audit trail:** rows written before this release name keys by their integer id; map them with `SELECT public_id FROM api_keys WHERE id = …` (see `docs/ops/agent-credential-runbook.md`).

### Notes
- Deploy the backend and the frontend together: a browser still running the previous bundle sends integer ids and gets 422 until it reloads.
- No new environment variables.

## [v0.88.0](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/releases/tag/v0.88.0) — 2026-10-02

One person's accounts can be linked so their private contexts have one owner, deleting a context now removes its vectors, and the plugin gains a skill that keeps memories current.

### Added
- **Linked accounts** ([#1784](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1784)): a CLI admin and an OAuth account of one person can be linked from Profile Settings. A private context is then open to every account linked to its creator, with the memories any of them wrote in it — in the context list, that context's memory list, recall, stats, tags and export. The link is proved by the browser session holding both accounts; nothing is linked by an email match. It widens ownership only: roles, workspace membership, `allowed_context_ids` and an API key's workspace scope stay the caller's own. Both accounts get an audit row and a security notice. `GET /api/v1/auth/me` returns `linked_user_ids`. See `docs/deployment.md`, *One person, two accounts*.
- **Orphan vector sweep** ([#1798](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1798)): a daily job (04:30 UTC) and `python -m src.cli.sweep_orphan_vectors` delete vector-store points no reader can reach — a memory point whose row is gone or was soft-deleted more than an hour ago, and a resource point whose context is. A point whose memory row is live is never deleted. The command is read-only by default; the scheduled run refuses to delete more than half of what it scanned. `ORPHAN_VECTOR_SWEEP_ENABLED=false` turns the scheduled run off.
- **`/kagura-memory:maintain`** ([#1800](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1800)): a plugin skill that reviews one context for memories to update, supersede, unpin or delete — ended and open follow-ups, pinned memories, and a topic the user names. It lists first and applies only the items the user picks by number; `dry-run` stops after the plan. `/kagura-memory:session-summary` gains a step that links or retires the memories a session replaced, and `/kagura-memory:session-start` points to the new command. The Codex skill carries both.

### Fixed
- **Deleting a context removes its vectors** ([#1798](https://github.com/kagura-ai/memory-cloud/issues/1798)): `delete_context` and `merge_contexts(delete_source=true)` remove the context's points from every `kagura_memories*` collection once the soft-delete is committed. Until now the points were kept and nothing removed them later, so the vector count drifted above the live memory count. Workspace deletion now covers the per-model collections too.

### Changed
